### BUG-056 — Residual gap at the bottom of the merged swap sheet ✅ **FIXED (`641aa3d`, device-confirmed August 6, 2026) — it was NEVER a padding defect**

- **Surface:** mobile `SwapMealSheet.tsx` (both modes), reached from the Plan Review meal row.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — commit `641aa3d` (WS9 3f-4b), local, device-confirmed by Hans August 6, 2026.** Surfaced by Hans's device test of WS9 3d Part 4, July 30, 2026; probed as **B3** and deliberately not fixed at that time.
- ✅ **ROOT CAUSE VERIFIED, and it was in NONE of the places three rounds of investigation looked.** The sheet is a transparent `<Modal>`. **Expo SDK 54 forces Android edge-to-edge**, `app.json`'s android block is `{}`, and **no sheet set `navigationBarTranslucent`** — so on Android gesture-nav the Modal stopped *above* the navigation bar. Backdrop and sheet both failed to cover that strip, and the Plan Review screen showed through it. **Fix: `statusBarTranslucent` + `navigationBarTranslucent` on the Modal**, after which the existing `paddingBottom: insets.bottom + Spacing[3]` becomes the correct single safe-area reservation.
- ⚠️ **THE DIAGNOSTIC MOVE THAT CRACKED IT, AND IT IS THE TRANSFERABLE LESSON: *a padding error cannot reveal the underlying screen.* ** Wrong padding pushes content up while the backdrop still covers everything. **The only way the screen below shows *beneath* the sheet edge is if the Modal itself does not reach the screen bottom.** That single observation ruled out every layout hypothesis at once. ⚠️ **TWO PRIOR HYPOTHESES WERE WRONG AND BOTH WERE CONFIDENT:** (1) CC's structural argument in 3f-4b — that removing the import cards from the bottom of the scroll would eliminate it — was reasonable, shipped, and **falsified by the next device test**; (2) chat-Claude's **double-inset** hypothesis (a `SafeAreaView` above also reserving `insets.bottom`) was **refuted by measurement** — `plan/[id].tsx` has zero `SafeAreaView` and zero insets consumers, and a Modal is a portal, so ancestor padding could not reach it regardless.
- ⚠️ **BLAST RADIUS — SPAWNED BUG-062.** Every other bottom-sheet `Modal` in the app shares the missing flag: `AddMealsSheet`, `AddMealToPlanSheet`, `PlanPreviewModal`, and possibly more. **CC correctly scope-fenced and did not touch them.** See BUG-062.
- **Symptom:** a small gap at the bottom of the sheet. **Cosmetic — Hans's read: *"it's not confusing."*** No functional impact.
- **⚠️ THE INTERESTING PART IS THAT IT SHOULD NOT EXIST.** The merged sheet's layout is **byte-identical to `FindSimilarSheet`'s**, which was the **gap-FREE** one — its `flex justify:flex-end, maxHeight:90%` was specifically chosen over `ChangeMealSheet`'s `position:absolute; height:85%` **because the latter had a bottom-gap bug.** **So the known-good layout is producing a gap anyway**, which means the cause is somewhere other than the sheet's own style block — safe-area insets, the new mode-header height, a parent container, or a device-specific inset.
- **⚠️ CC STOPPED RATHER THAN GUESSING, WHICH WAS THE CORRECT CALL.** The scope guard said fix only if it was a one-line layout adjustment. **The cause is not visible in source**, so any change would have been a speculative edit to the shell of a merge that had just landed. **Fixing a symptom you cannot locate is how you get a second bug.**
- **What diagnosis needs:** on-device inspection with the element inspector — measure where the gap actually is (below the sheet, inside the content container, or safe-area padding), and compare against the pre-merge `FindSimilarSheet` on the same device to confirm it is genuinely new.
- **Priority:** **P3.** ⚠️ **Judged against Hans's standing bar — *"a friend thinks it's amazing"*, strong first impression, not work-in-progress feel** — a visible gap is the kind of small wrongness that reads as unfinished, so it should not ride all the way to go-live. **P3 means later, not never.**
- **Target:** WS9 **3f** (which owns meal/dish detail and the shared `ask-kiwi.tsx` creator, so it will be in this sheet anyway) or a WS9 polish sweep.
- ⚠️ **ORPHANED — NO SUB-BLOCK OWNS IT (flagged August 5, 2026).** The target above predates the 3f split into 3f-1…3f-5. **3f-4 is the LAST sub-block that opens `SwapMealSheet.tsx`** — it is the swap-sheet wire block — while 3f-5 is net-new backend and 3g is a different surface. **If 3f-4 does not pick it up, it falls to a "WS9 polish sweep" that is not currently on the roadmap.** ⚠️ **This is a SCOPE call and belongs to Hans — deliberately not assigned here.** Note it needs on-device inspection, which makes it a natural pairing with 3f-4's device test rather than a code task.
- ✅ **RE-CONFIRMED BY HANS ON DEVICE, August 5, 2026:** *"the small gap at the bottom of that component, it's real and I'm glad we're tracking it somewhere to resolve. it's not a stopper by any means, but a good polish item to keep on the list."* ⚠️ **P3 stands and the bug is REAL — this is a second independent device observation, not a re-reading of the first.** **Hans will raise scheduling with the 3f-4 chat directly; it is NOT assigned here.** **Keep it on the list — do not close it as cosmetic-and-forgotten, and do not let it drop out of tracking if 3f-4 declines it.**
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-018 (the sheet merge) · the Part 4 lossless-merge checklist.

### BUG-055 — Any preferences save re-stamps `dietaryUpdatedAt`, so a household-size change marks EVERY plan dietarily stale ✅ **FIXED (`6b6e46b`, device-confirmed July 30)** — the fix location the first analysis missed

- **Surface:** mobile `app/preferences.tsx` (whole-form save) → `PATCH /me/preferences` (`me.ts:202-207,799-802`) → `computeDietaryStale` → the Plan Review dietary banner.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** (`6b6e46b`, WS9 3d Part 3c-2) and **device-confirmed** July 30, 2026 — changing household size and spice tolerance no longer raise the banner; adding an allergy still does. Surfaced by CC during Part 3c as a consequence of its own B5 work. **Pre-existing — not introduced by 3c** — but 3c changed it from occasional to constant.
- **✅ THE FIX: a server-side conditional stamp.** `PATCH /me/preferences` now stamps `dietaryUpdatedAt` only when a dietary field is present **AND its value differs from the stored row** (order-insensitive on the array fields — reordering `["Nut-free","Dairy-free"]` is not a change). The stored row is fetched for the comparison. **No mobile change at all** — the client still sends the whole form; the server decides. On a first-ever save with no existing row, comparison is against schema defaults so an initial dietary entry still stamps correctly.
- **Symptom (user-visible, once B2 lands):** the user changes **household size** (or any non-dietary preference). Every saved plan now displays *"your dietary preferences have changed since this plan was made."* **A false alarm on a safety-adjacent, trust-bearing banner.**
- **Root cause (VERIFIED, from CC's own A1 probe of the same code path):** `PATCH /me/preferences` stamps `dietaryUpdatedAt` **iff any of `{allergiesAndAvoidances, dietaryNotes, eatingStyles, pickyAvoidances}` is PRESENT in the payload** — presence, not change. The client's `handleSave` spreads the **whole form**, so all four are always present on every save. The stamp therefore fires on every save regardless of what was edited.
- **⚠️ TWO CORRECT CHANGES COMBINED INTO A VISIBLE REGRESSION THAT NEITHER WOULD HAVE PRODUCED ALONE.** **B5** (auto-save, D-WS9-091) makes the whole-form PATCH fire on *every keystroke-settled edit* instead of on an explicit Save tap. **B2** fixed the client refetch gap so the banner now actually appears when the server says stale. Before 3c the bug was latent twice over — saves were rare, and the banner didn't refetch anyway. **After 3c it is a constant, visible false positive.** Neither B2 nor B5 is wrong; the interaction is.
- **⚠️ MECHANISM CORRECTION (CC, Part 3c-2) — the symptom description implied something false.** *Spice tolerance* was never in the stamp set; `spiceTolerance` is already excluded. **The trigger is that the four dietary keys are ALWAYS PRESENT on a whole-form save**, so a presence-based stamp fires on every save no matter which field the user actually touched. **Same defect, same fix, corrected mechanism** — and worth keeping, because "spice tolerance is in the stamp set" would have sent a future reader to the wrong list.
- **✅ THE AUDIT FOUND A FIX LOCATION THE FIRST ANALYSIS DID NOT SEARCH, AND IT WAS SMALLER AND MORE CORRECT — this is the durable part of this entry.** CC's proposal: *"client sends only changed fields (needs form diffing = the state restructure the B5 guard forbids here)"* — which correctly concludes the fix is out of scope, but only because it considered one location. **A server-side conditional stamp — stamp iff a dietary field is present AND its value DIFFERS from the stored row — is a small, local change requiring no client work at all**, and it sits where the standing architecture invariant says it belongs: *server owns all math, attribution and structure; mobile renders and never recomputes.* **Deciding "who computes changed-ness" is exactly the judgment the 3b P1 already proved should not live on the client.** ✅ **VERIFIED BY THE BUILD:** the fix landed server-side in one commit with **zero mobile changes** and three new server tests. ⚠️ **The generalizable lesson is not "the audit was right" — it is that "no fix is in scope" is a claim about WHERE the search looked.** CC's conclusion was sound for the location it considered (the client) and wrong as a statement about the system. **When a report says a fix is out of scope, ask which layer it searched.**
- **Priority:** **P2.** **Target:** ✅ **WS9 3d Part 3c-2, shipped.** A false staleness banner is the kind of wrong that erodes trust in the correct ones, which is why it was fixed before Part 4 rather than bundled to WS10.
- **⚠️ Tests added (3):** a dietary change stamps · a non-dietary change does **not** · array reordering does **not**. **The middle one is the regression guard** — it is the assertion that would have caught this originally.
- **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-091** (the auto-save ruling that amplified it) · **D-WS9-089** (a *different* defect on this same save path — server-owned keys in an echoed payload; **do not conflate the two**) · **D-WS9-013** (the dietary-staleness feature this undermines) · **R-3d-9** in `kiwi_ux_redesign_spec.md` §12.

### BUG-053 — Surprise-me "More options" returns the plan just rejected — TWO independent causes, and fixing either alone does not fix the symptom ✅ FIXED (`74edb64` + `0b515e8`, device-confirmed July 29)

- **Surface:** mobile `app/wizard-results.tsx` (the auto-expand effect) **and** the `wizard.surprise.generate` prompt input. Two surfaces, two causes, one symptom.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED.** Cause 1 at `74edb64` (Group 2), cause 2 at `0b515e8` (Group 3, Parts B + F). Logged and closed the same day, July 29, 2026 — **surfaced by Hans during device testing**, from his own reading of the server log. **Device-confirmed:** re-rolls return distinct plans.
- **Symptom (user-visible):** tapping **Surprise Me again** re-displays the plan the user just rejected. Hans: *"going back reloads the options and then gives the same plan again."*
- **⚠️ CAUSE 1 — A CLIENT RACE. One tap fired two concurrent requests, 2ms apart:**
  ```
  10:41:11.218  POST /wizard/expand       → 200 in 123ms,   NO store_compose_summary
  10:41:11.220  POST /wizard/surprise-me  → 200 in 11478ms, real AI call, $0.031362
  10:41:22.431  wizard_draft_supersede    superseded: 1
  ```
  The 123ms expand is a **content-hash draft reuse** (no compose summary means nothing was composed), and it **beats the new generation by eleven seconds** — so the rejected plan is re-rendered long before its replacement exists. **VERIFIED by code read, not timing inference:** `handleMoreOptions` calls `setAttempt(n+1)`, which re-arms the once-per-attempt guard on a render where `surpriseMutation.reset()` has **not yet cleared `candidates`** — so the auto-expand effect fires `runOpenDraft(candidates[0])` on the stale rejected candidate. CC confirmed that effect is the **sole** concurrent-expand source on this path.
- **⚠️ CAUSE 2 — THE MODEL IS NEVER TOLD WHAT IT JUST PRODUCED. VERIFIED by measurement:** both surprise calls logged **`inputTokens: 8139` — byte-identical**. `planningContext.recentPlanNames` is populated only from *persisted recent plans*; the current session's shown-and-rejected candidates never reach it. Same prompt + same preferences + no exclusion → naturally similar plan.
- **⚠️ THE TWO CAUSES ARE INDEPENDENT.** Removing the race makes the re-roll show *the new plan*; without the exclusion, that new plan may still be near-identical. **Neither fix alone closes the user-visible complaint.** This is the distinguishing feature of this bug and the reason it was not treated as one defect with one fix.
- **The fixes:** **Part A** (`74edb64`) deletes the auto-expand effect entirely — surprise now lands on an expanded card and expands only on an explicit proceed, so the race is gone **by construction, not by guard**. **Part B** threads a session-scoped exclusion list (every plan shown this session, not just the last) into `recentPlanNames` / `recentMeals` via a new **optional** request body, and widens the existing repetition instruction — `:300` v2→v3.
- **⚠️ THE STANDARD WIZARD SHARES CAUSE 2** (`wizard.set_preferences.generate`, `aiPrompts.ts:895`) — its "More options ↺" can also re-surface a just-shown candidate. Milder (three candidates, not one). **Hans ruled it in scope** (Part F, "close it all out").
- **⚠️ TELL KIWI DOES NOT SHARE IT and is deliberately out of scope** (Hans's ruling, July 29). `wizard.directed.generate` (`aiPrompts.ts:239`) has **no re-roll** — its "More options" does `router.back()` to the form. Reaching the same plans requires resubmitting the same text, and returning the same answer to the same question is defensible determinism, not staleness. **Do not "fix" this by symmetry on a future pass.**
- **⚠️ AN IDENTIFICATION ERROR HERE COST A ROUND-TRIP, AND IT WAS CHAT-CLAUDE'S.** A Phase 0 report labelled `:239` "directed generate" and left `:895` unidentified; the follow-up prompt then read "directed generate" as the standard wizard and instructed CC to **change `:239` and not `:895` — the exact inverse of the intent.** CC caught the contradiction, stopped, and refused to guess. Confirmed independently from two sources: the canonical prompt inventory (`wizard.directed.generate` = *"given a parsed Tell Kiwi intent"*) and the device log (`POST /wizard/build-plans` → `promptKey: "wizard.set_preferences.generate"`). **Prompt constants are not self-describing from a line number; identify by key and by call site.**
- **⚠️ CACHE ASYMMETRY BETWEEN THE TWO PROMPTS — carry this forward.** `wizard.surprise.generate` runs **uncached** (`runAICall` caching is opt-in; the surprise call site passes no cache option, matching its logged `0/0`). `wizard.set_preferences.generate` logged **`cacheCreationInputTokens: 3875`** — a live cached prefix. Per §7, exclusion **data** must sit below the boundary; editing instruction text above it invalidates the cache until it re-warms. **A conclusion drawn about one of these prompts does not transfer to the other.**
- **Priority:** P2. **Target:** WS9 3c follow-up, Groups 2 and 3.
- **✅ THE FIX AS BUILT, and one asymmetry it left.** `sessionExclusion.ts` accumulates shown plan titles + meal titles (keyed on the arriving candidate, deduped by title) and threads them through an **optional** request body on both routes. **Surprise** merges plan titles → `recentPlanNames` and meal titles → `recentMeals`. **Build-plans cannot** — D-WS9-073 **strips `recentMeals`** on that route in favour of `recentRotation` — so *both* fold into `recentPlanNames`. ⚠️ **A field named "plan names" now carries meal titles on one of the two routes.** CC deliberately left `recentRotation` alone because its consumers were unvetted, which was the right conservative call. **→ D-WS9-087.**
- **✅ `RecentMeal.source: "planned"` was ruled safe BY GREP, not by reasoning.** The concern was that labelling a *rejected* meal "planned" would invert its meaning if anything read `recentMeals` as a preference signal. Verified: the field's **only** consumer is the surprise prompt's steer-away instruction — build-plans and build-from-text strip it, and the store-exclusion `recentMealIds` uses a separate local fetch. Repetition-avoidance only → `"planned"` is harmless, **no type change, D-WS9-074 untouched.** ⚠️ **If a second consumer of `recentMeals` is ever added, this ruling expires.**
- **📝 DEVICE OBSERVATION, July 29 — NOT LOGGED AS A DEFECT (Hans: no action needed).** Post-fix, **plans are distinct but individual meals still recur across them.** Two explanations fit and neither has been separated: (1) the `recentPlanNames` overload above — the `:895` instruction targets *plan names*, so meal-level avoidance is weaker on the standard-wizard route; (2) **by design** — D-WS9-073 made repeat-avoidance an **AI-judged soft nudge**, not a hard exclusion, and the best-burger principle deliberately biases toward letting loved meals recur. **Hans is observing rather than acting.** ⚠️ **If this is ever escalated, separate (1) from (2) before proposing a fix** — tightening a soft nudge that is working as specified would be a regression against D-WS9-073.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-052 (the supersede at 10:41:22 is the same collision, arising here from an *ordinary* tap rather than a deliberate one) · D-WS9-073 (the soft-nudge rotation design that explanation (2) rests on) · D-WS9-086 / D-WS9-087 (the two divergences this fix accepted) · D-WS9-074 (Zod-boundary work these bundle with) · the best-burger principle.

### BUG-052 — A card tapped mid-generation creates a draft that the same generation's end-of-route supersede then nulls: save and activate both fail 422 "malformed" ✅ FIXED (`9a7f59d`, device-confirmed July 29)

- **Surface:** server — `supersedeUnconsumedWizardDrafts` (`wizardExpansion.ts:547`) and `readAndFinalizeWizardDraft` (`wizardFinalize.ts`). The client is not implicated.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** at `9a7f59d` (WS9 3c follow-up Group 1), **device-confirmed July 29, 2026** — a mid-generation card tap now commits cleanly. Logged and closed the same day.
- **Symptom (user-visible):** *"when I tried to save a plan it said draft malformed."* Both **Use This Week** and **Save for Later** failed identically, twice each, with 422.
- **⚠️ ROOT CAUSE — VERIFIED BY READ-ONLY DB PROBE.** This is the strongest evidence class used on this project so far, and it was mandatory rather than optional (see below).
  ```
  09:27:46.3  POST /wizard/build-plans   starts       (18443ms)
  09:28:01.7  POST /wizard/expand        starts       → 200, 3/3 store-bound   [mid-generation]
  09:28:03.098  draft 04aa7024 createdAt              [1.7s inside the expand window]
  09:28:04.449  draft 04aa7024 updatedAt              [~130ms before the supersede log line]
  09:28:04.58 wizard_draft_supersede     superseded: 1
  09:28:07.3  POST .../activate          → 422 reason:"root"
  09:28:24.6  POST .../save              → 422 reason:"root"
  ```
  The probe found `wizardContentHash` **present** and `wizardDraftPayload` **null**. **The draft was written correctly; the supersede nulled it.** `supersedeUnconsumedWizardDrafts`'s `WHERE` was `{ userId, isWizardDraft: true, isArchived: false }` with **nothing scoping it by batch, generation start, or `createdAt`**, and it sets `wizardDraftPayload: DbNull`. **All 27 of this user's wizard drafts read `archived: true, payload: null`** — the blanket supersede's fingerprint across the account's entire history.
- **What `reason: "root"` means:** `readAndFinalizeWizardDraft` runs `WizardExpandedPlanDetailsSchema.safeParse(payload)` and maps issues via `i.path.join(".") || "root"`. An empty path means the value failed at the **top level** — exactly what `safeParse(null)` yields. So the "malformed payload" error was reporting the supersede's null, not a bad write.
- **⚠️ THE TWO CANDIDATE STORIES WERE NOT COMPETING — ONE CAUSES THE OTHER.** Diagnosis considered (a) a supersede collision and (b) a genuinely malformed payload written by the mid-generation expand. **(a) produces (b).** Chat-Claude initially framed these as alternatives and later narrowed to two when `reason: "root"` implied a third; **the probe settled all readings in one pass.** ⚠️ **Unlike BUG-051, no single fix was correct under both stories** — scoping the supersede would have masked the symptom while leaving a bad-write bug alive. **That asymmetry is the test for whether a probe is optional: if one fix serves every candidate story, build it; if not, instrument first.**
- **Why WS9 3c introduced it:** Block 4b-3 moved `supersedeUnconsumedWizardDrafts` off the four consume sites onto the three generate routes (the BUG-047 fix). That was safe under the old R5 flow, where **expand only ever ran after generation finished**. 3c moved expand to card-tap, which can fire **mid-stream** — so draft creation now precedes the supersede that ends the same generation. **A correct fix in one block created the precondition for this one two blocks later.**
- **⚠️ HANS'S RULING — TOLERATE, FIX THE TECH.** Verbatim: *"I intentionally clicked during the plan option generation to push on the build. like if a user just goes in and wants that plan first thing before anything loads, the app should behave."* **Do NOT gate the UI. Do NOT make cards untappable during generation.** Making the user wait for the machine is friction debt (§9).
- **The fix (`9a7f59d`):** `supersedeUnconsumedWizardDrafts` takes a `createdBefore` cutoff = `generationStartedAt`, captured at the top of each generate handler and threaded through `commitGeneratedBatch` at all four call sites. Only drafts created **strictly before the generation began** are archived; a mid-stream draft survives. Regression proved by reverting the clause.
- **⚠️ WATCH ITEM — CLOCK SKEW.** The cutoff is an **app-server** timestamp compared against a **Postgres** `createdAt` on Neon. Margin is large in practice (~8s between generation start and first streamed card), so this is not a rebuild — but if a mid-generation tap ever fails again after this fix, **check clock skew before re-deriving the mechanism.** A batch-identity scope rather than a time scope would be immune; it was not built because it needs a batch id on drafts.
- **⚠️ A SECOND, PRE-EXISTING DEFECT SURFACED FROM THE SAME PROBE AND IS NOT THE REPORTED BUG.** `readAndFinalizeWizardDraft` loads by id with **no `isArchived` filter**, and archived drafts retain `isWizardDraft: true`, so they pass the ownership check and reach `safeParse(null)`. **Therefore "draft malformed" was the error for *any* superseded draft, not only a raced one** — scoping the supersede fixes the collision but not the message. Fixed in the same commit: the finalize path returns `status: "archived"` **before** the parse, activate and save map it to **409 `{reason:"archived"}`** (409 over 404, which would have tripped the mobile dropped-201 recovery), and the client shows *"this plan is no longer available"* instead of a corruption error. ⚠️ **CC reported this inside a mechanism explanation rather than escalating it as a finding** — the analysis was right, the escalation was incomplete.
- **⚠️ THE NAMED REACHABLE PATH WAS PROVEN UNREACHABLE, AND THE FIX WAS STILL CORRECT TO BUILD.** Chat-Claude hypothesised the rehydrate → expand → commit path as the route to an archived draft, and instructed CC to skip the fix if it proved unreachable. It **is** unreachable — expand's content-hash reuse filters `isArchived: false` and falls through to regenerate. **CC built it anyway and was right to:** the *general* class of committing an archived draft is reachable, and **BUG-052 itself is the proof-by-example.** The instruction conflated a specific path with the class. **CC disclosed the override explicitly, which is what made it acceptable.**
- **Priority:** P2 — not P1, because it needs a deliberate mid-generation tap to reach on the standard wizard. ⚠️ **But BUG-053's log shows the same supersede collision arising from an ordinary "More options" tap with no deliberate racing at all**, so the collision class is *not* an edge case even though this instance was provoked.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-047 (the 4b-3 supersede move that created the precondition) · BUG-053 (same collision, ordinary tap) · BUG-030 / D-WS9-035 (the draft-lifecycle idempotency work this protects) · D-WS9-032 (the flow that moved expand to card-tap).

### BUG-050 — Streamed build-plans stored raw un-reconciled candidates: rehydrated batches carried `m1` aliases, so every slot demoted to live AI ✅ FIXED (`fc9e28a`)

- **Surface:** `POST /api/wizard/build-plans` streamed path → `commitGeneratedBatch` (`wizard.ts:686`); consumed by the rehydrate → expand path.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** at `fc9e28a` (July 28, 2026), device-verified. Logged and fixed in the same block. **Self-reported by CC, unasked**, while refuting a different hypothesis.
- **⚠️ ROOT CAUSE — VERIFIED (CC, from its own code).** The streamed path committed `finalCandidates` — the raw AI output. `sendCandidate` reconciles a **copy** for the wire and never mutates `finalCandidates`, so what went to the client was correct while what went to the database was not. A rehydrated batch therefore carried `m1`-style aliases in `storeSlots` instead of real `Meal.id`s; expand has no `aliasToId` map to translate them, `composeStoreMealDetails("m1")` misses, and **every slot demotes to live generation**. The **buffered** path stored reconciled candidates and was correct throughout — the two paths had silently diverged.
- **The cost of the defect, measured:** a rehydrated expand ran 3 `wizard.candidate.expand` + 8 `nutrition.ingredient_estimate` + 3 `finalize_steps` — **~$0.27 and ~40s per plan**, against ~$0 and ~2s when bound.
- **The fix:** accumulate the reconciled copies `sendCandidate` already builds into a separate index-ordered array and commit **that**. ⚠️ **Deliberately did NOT mutate `finalCandidates`** — doing so would have silently changed what the `storeSlotsMarked` metric measures and destroyed comparability with the historical baseline, which is what made it safe to ship this fix alongside the BUG-049 instrumentation in one block.
- **⚠️ THE TEST THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT COULDN'T, STRUCTURALLY.** `previousOptions.test.ts`'s fixture omitted `storeSlots`, so its round-trip `deepEqual` asserted over objects that never had the field. **Same shape as the latency block's parser gap** — a fuzz test that passed on accumulated prefixes while production broke on fragments. Closed with a `storeSlots`-bearing fixture plus a streamed-path server test, and **the pre-fix failure was proved by reverting the one line** (`AssertionError: 'm1' !== 'store-1'`) rather than asserted. **A test written against a fixture that omits the field under test is not a test of that field.**
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-049 (misattributed to this defect's symptom) · D-WS9-072 (the batch persistence this corrupts) · the latency block (`32c28e3`, the prior instance of the same fixture-gap pattern).

### BUG-047 — `supersedeUnconsumedWizardDrafts` archives ALL of a user's unconsumed drafts on any commit — a third draft-clearing path against D-WS9-072 ✅ FIXED (`50b3e11`)

- **Surface:** Wizard draft lifecycle, server-side (`wizardExpansion.ts:552`, called from the activate/save paths in `wizard.ts` including the idempotent-return branches at `:1434` / `:1584` / `:1659` / `:1789`).
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** at `50b3e11` (July 28, 2026, Block 4b-3 Part 1a), pushed and device-verified. Logged July 27, 2026 — surfaced **unasked** by CC during the Block 4b-2 Phase 0 draft-lifecycle trace, while investigating BUG-038.
- **✅ THE FIX (July 28, 2026).** `supersedeUnconsumedWizardDrafts` was **removed from all four consume sites** (`wizard.ts:1468`, `:1618`, `:1693`, `:1823`) and **moved to the three generate routes**, so the code now literally implements D-WS9-072's single rule: **generation clears, nothing else does.** The idempotent-return branches got a **scoped self-archive** (`updateMany where id = draftId`) rather than the blanket supersede — they still need to retire the draft they themselves consumed, without touching siblings. ⚠️ **No session id was added**, per the entry's own instruction below; the refined ruling made the plumbing unnecessary rather than merely unwanted.
- **Verified behavior change on device:** expand A → expand B → activate A now leaves **B intact** as a resumable unconsumed draft until the next generation. Hans ruled this correct and intended (see D-WS9-084's sibling ruling on the batch) — *"it's ok if they see prior results and the prior results included the one they used."* Marking the already-used option is **WS9 3c polish**, explicitly **not** a second clearing act.
- **⚠️ The mobile-cache contributor noted below was NOT fixed here** and has since recurred on a sibling query — see **BUG-051** (`last-batch` never invalidated on generation). **Fix both cache gaps together in 3c**, preferring invalidation on the generation mutation's success over screen-specific calls.
- **Symptom (user-visible):** previously-viewed plan options resurface on wizard re-entry **inconsistently**. Hans: *"I noticed old candidates showing up when I re-ran the wizard, but it's not happening consistently."*
- **⚠️ ROOT CAUSE — VERIFIED, and the intermittency is deterministic, not random.** The supersede `WHERE` is scoped to **`userId` only** — no draft id, no batch id, no session id (`wizardExpansion.ts:553-557`). So **committing any plan archives every other unconsumed draft the user holds**, including unrelated orphans from other sessions. A previewed draft survives only in the window **between previewing it and the next commit of anything**. Whether a commit intervened is the entire explanation for "sometimes it comes back." (The *other* half of the intermittency is the mount-gate behavior recorded on BUG-038 — the two compound.)
- **⚠️ THIS IS A DEFECT AGAINST A STANDING RULING, not an open design question.** **D-WS9-072 ruled that generation is the ONLY clearing act** — refined July 27 to a single rule: *"user generates plans, sees results… if they click Generate Plans again, clear the prior results and write the new ones."* **Drafts survive saving a plan.** Activate/save firing supersede breaks that half of the ruling directly.
- **⚠️ DO NOT FIX THIS BY ADDING A SESSION ID.** The code carries an in-line note describing the broad scope as intentional-pending-a-session-id (`wizardExpansion.ts:538-542`), and **D-WS9-035 originally deferred exactly that**. **D-WS9-072 SUPERSEDED it and ruled the session-id plumbing out of scope.** Under the refined model the plumbing is not merely unwanted but *unnecessary*: if generation always clears and rewrites, **"all unconsumed drafts" IS the last run by construction** — there is nothing to disambiguate. The fix is to change **WHEN supersede fires** (regenerate-only), not to narrow whose drafts it catches. **Reading the code comment without reading D-WS9-072 will lead a future chat to build the thing that was ruled against.**
- **Adjacent finding, same trace (not a separate bug, but fix-adjacent):** `(userId, wizardContentHash)` is a plain `@@index`, **not `@@unique`** (`schema.prisma:714`), so the expand reuse-branch is a read-then-create with no uniqueness guard. This is *why* four expands of one candidate produced four rows: aggressive archival means there is usually no `isArchived:false` draft left to match, so every expand creates anew. **The two interact** — changing supersede scope without considering the missing guard will change draft-accumulation behavior.
- **Also noted (contributor, not cause):** the mobile `["wizard","drafts"]` query is invalidated in exactly one place — after "Get new results" (`wizard.tsx:360`) — and **not** after expand/create/activate. `staleTime:0` refetch-on-mount mostly covers it, but re-entry first-paints against the stale cached list. Fold into the same fix rather than logging separately.
- **Priority:** P2. **Target: Block 4b-3** (draft-batch persistence, D-WS9-072's build) — the same machinery that block rewrites, so fixing it elsewhere means touching draft lifecycle twice.
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-072 (the governing ruling — **read before fixing**) · D-WS9-035 (superseded; the session-id deferral) · BUG-038 (closed as overtaken; this is the durable finding from its trace) · BUG-030 (content-hash idempotency, Block 1) · D-WS9-083 (speculative-expand feasibility).

### BUG-042 — Cooking/prep step text shows raw decimals (`1.25`, `1.5`, `.333`) instead of fractions (1¼, 1½, ⅓) ✅ FIXED (`7433d0c`)

- **Surface:** Cook Mode / Meal Detail / Prep step text — the rendered instruction strings, NOT the grocery list (grocery was fixed at `13f48c3`).
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED July 20, 2026 (`7433d0c`, pushed).** Fixed at generation (option c); option a demoted to optional and not built. **⚠️ See the RESOLUTION block immediately below — the diagnostic figures in the original entry (60/360, 39/21) were STALE and were corrected against the live catalog before the fix.** Original entry retained verbatim as history.
- **Priority:** P2 for existing meals. **⚠️ P1-BY-DEADLINE for the generation half — see the gate below.**
- **Target:** its own tightly-gated block (split from the post-Block-3 latency batch by Hans's ruling, July 19, 2026, because the generation-side fix edits the `finalize_steps` prompt body — the same prompt the catalog harness uses).

**✅ RESOLUTION (`7433d0c`, July 20, 2026) — fixed at generation; the diagnosis was corrected mid-block.**
- **⚠️ THE TRIAGE FIGURES WERE STALE (fourth such correction this arc, §27).** Phase 0 re-ran the actual detector over the committed public catalog rather than trusting the snapshot: **314 dish steps · 44 with a decimal · 68 decimal spans · 63 matched (glyph correctly) · 4 by-design (time/dimension/temperature, correctly no ref) · 1 genuinely unmatched.** NOT "60/360, 39 covered / 21 not." The residual is a **single** span — `"0.5 cup pasta water"` — where the amount attaches to a **cooking byproduct, not a DishIngredient**, so the name-anchored matcher cannot resolve it. One cause, not several. (Same class as the 44-meal store count → 25, the "2 appliance entries dropped" → never dropped, and `STABLE_GENERATE_PREFIX` 2,453 → 2,888: canon carried a figure the artifact disagreed with.)
- **⚠️ DECIMALS ARE 100% MODEL-EMITTED (verified, not assumed).** Every persistence hop traced: `stepTextRaw` is written verbatim from the model's `steps[].text` (harness `mergeSteps → materializeMeal`; live `wizardActivation.ts:309`). No code multiplies, converts, or rewrites step text. The model copied the ingredient's `quantity: Float` (correct as structured data) into prose. Because the source is the model, a generation instruction can solve it — which a code-side widening could not.
- **THE FIX — option (c), a REPLACEMENT of one sentence in BOTH finalize bodies** (`aiPrompts.ts:1029` wizard + `storeFillPrompts.ts:112` harness; byte-identical, so both changed together or they diverge per §10). The single amount-formatting instruction — *"…reuse those numbers in the steps"* — was **extended, not layered**, to: *"…reuse those numbers in the steps, written as natural cooking measures — use fraction glyphs (½, ¼, ¾, ⅓, 1½) for non-whole amounts, never decimals (write '1½ cups', not '1.5 cups')."* Post-edit re-read confirmed exactly one amount-formatting instruction remains (the second `fraction glyph` hit in `aiPrompts.ts:1396` is a different, pre-existing prompt).
- **⚠️ (a) `deriveAmountRefs` widening — DEMOTED, NOT BUILT.** The detector already parses decimals *and* vulgar fractions (`½¼¾`, coalesced `1½`); there was no detection gap to widen. The only residual is resolution against non-ingredient nouns, which no widening resolves. CC declined to claim a widening that doesn't address the residual, and was ratified. **(c) fixes the residual for free anyway:** the generation instruction now renders *"½ cup pasta water"* in persisted text whether or not a matcher can bind it.
- **⚠️ (b) render-time regex — stays FORBIDDEN, and P0.4 proved why with the actual mechanism.** Step text is authored at base servings and is **not scaled** (D-WS9-050); only the `amountRef` carries a structured quantity the client multiplies by the servings ratio. A regex swapping `1.5→1½` in the *text* would render an **unscaled** glyph that looks authoritative and is wrong at any non-default servings. The `amountRef` path is the only vehicle that both glyphs *and* scales.
- **VERIFICATION (§27 — confirmed, not assumed):** dry-run smoke on a fresh Chicken Tikka Masala → **15 fraction glyphs, 0 raw decimals, and 15/15 glyph spans resolved by the real `deriveAmountRefs`** (the detector parsed `½`/`1½` and bound each to an ingredient — the load-bearing check, since a fix that glyphs but loses the scaling ref would be a worse trade). Live path confirmed: wizard save calls the same detector (`wizardActivation.ts:291`); the re-seed bumped `wizard.candidate.finalize_steps` to serve the glyph instruction.
- **⚠️ CACHED PREFIX re-measured with a REAL `count_tokens` (not estimated — the char estimate proved untrustworthy earlier this arc):** `STABLE_FINALIZE_PREFIX` **3,106 → 3,162** (+56), clears the 2048 floor at **+54.4%**. In-file comment updated to 3,162. Generate prefix untouched (2,888).
- **BLAST RADIUS — this touched the LIVE WIZARD path, not just the harness.** Every user who saves a wizard plan now gets fraction glyphs in their steps. An improvement, no functional risk (detector + client already handle glyphs), but a live-path change by design.
- **SEQUENCING — shipped BEFORE the scale run deliberately:** all ~1,133 meals are now authored with glyphs in persisted text from the start, rather than persisting decimals and leaning on the ref overlay. Fixing after would have meant a catalog-wide re-derivation or a text rewrite (and rewrite is the forbidden option b). The 20 pilot rows already sit at ~full coverage (1 residual), closable with a small ref re-derivation, no text rewrite.
- **LOGGED, not chased (per ruling):** the `"0.5 cup pasta water"` non-ingredient-noun residual. The generation fix renders it as `"½ cup"` regardless; no matcher work pursued.
- **Verify:** typecheck exit 0; suite **1330/1328/0/2** (baseline held). Commit `7433d0c`, 2 files (3 insertions, 3 deletions), staged by explicit path.


- **Symptom (Hans, device test):** *"it's displaying things like .333 and .25 instead of 1/3 and 1/4 measurements in the cooking instructions/steps."*

**Root cause — a MIX, and the split is the whole story (VERIFIED, counts from DB):**
- The decimal is **baked into persisted step text**. Of **360 dish-owned steps** across the 20 catalog meals, **60 (16.7%)** contain a decimal in `stepTextRaw` — e.g. *"Add the 1.25 pounds ground beef"*, *"1.5 tablespoons butter"*. These strings are written by the **finalize-steps AI**, which echoes ingredient quantities (themselves stored as decimals like 1.25 / 1.5).
- **The formatter exists and already works.** `lib/format/quantity.ts` (`formatQuantity` / `formatNeedGlyph`, from `13f48c3`) maps a number → vulgar-fraction glyph. `buildAmountRefSegments` (`amountSegments.ts:55`) swaps an `amountRef` span for the glyph, and is consumed by Meal Detail (`meal/[id].tsx:262`) and Cook Mode (`CookSessionView.tsx:98`).
- **So it is NOT "the formatter isn't called."** Of the 60 decimal steps, **39 ARE `amountRef`-covered and glyph correctly at render**. The residue is the **21 that are NOT ref-covered** — `deriveAmountRefs` (materialize-time) failed to capture the number→ingredient→unit span, so there's no ref to trigger replacement, and the lossless `highlightQuantities` fallback **deliberately preserves the literal**. Those 21 are what Hans is seeing.
- ⚠️ **INFERRED (not verified):** that the 21 uncovered steps are *exactly* what was on screen. The count is solid; the attribution to the specific observed instances is not.

**⚠️ THE SCALE-RUN GATE — this is why the entry exists rather than a chat note:**
The **generation-side defect is unfixed**, and the scale run will write **~1,133 meals** through the same `finalize_steps` prompt. At the pilot's 16.7% rate that is **~190 meals carrying decimal step text**, of which a similar minority would be un-fixable client-side. **Fixing this after the scale run means a regeneration or a text backfill across the whole catalog; fixing it before costs a prompt edit.** The 20 meals already written are affected and need remediation either way.

**Three candidate fixes (Hans leaned a+c, July 19, 2026 — not finally ruled):**
- **(a) Widen `deriveAmountRefs` coverage** so more decimals become ref-covered → fixes existing catalog meals with **no regeneration**. Does not help decimals with no bindable ingredient.
- **(b) Text-level decimal→glyph pass at render** → catches everything including un-refable text, but **blunt**: risks converting a decimal that should stay decimal (temperature, percentage, a measurement that isn't a fraction). Hans leaned AGAINST.
- **(c) Fix at generation** — instruct `finalize_steps` to emit fractions/glyphs in step text → **cleanest long-term and the only one that protects the scale run**, but the 20 existing meals still need a backfill.

**⚠️ Build discipline when this block is commissioned:** (c) is a **prompt body edit** → §10 replace-don't-layer applies, and a **Phase 0 verbatim re-read + conflict scan** is mandatory before any Phase 1 edit (the line-824 lesson: layered contradictory instructions ship silently). This prompt is **shared with the catalog harness**, so an error propagates into every catalog meal written afterward.

- **Cross-ref:** `13f48c3` (the grocery-surface fraction-glyph fix that built the formatter — precedent, and proof this is small) · BUG-025-2 (⅛-ladder fractional rounding, the grocery sibling) · D-WS9-045 (the ~1,133-meal scale run this gates) · D-WS9-040 (catalog quality gate — this is a quality property the checklist does not currently assert) · D-WS9-049 (the post-Block-3 latency batch this was split OUT of).

### BUG-037 — Surprise Me should give ONE plan → straight to the draft screen (with "surprise me again" until save), NOT 3 candidates ✅ FIXED

- **Surface:** Wizard entry → **Surprise Me** path (distinct from **Use My Prefs**).
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** in Plan-Gen Arc Block 2 fix pass (`5c883e3`, device-verified July 17, 2026) — **built during the arc after all**, not deferred to Block 4. Surprise returns 1 candidate (`SURPRISE_CANDIDATE_COUNT`), mobile auto-expands to the draft screen (no 3-card picker), "Surprise Me again" re-rolls + supersedes the prior draft (reuses Block 1's dismiss primitive). Device test confirmed: single plan to draft, Save/Use present, clean re-roll with no orphan-draft pile-up across multiple re-rolls.
- **Priority:** P2 (was). **Resolved in:** Block 2 fix pass (Fix 5).
- **The two paths are supposed to differ (Hans's ruling):**
  - **Use My Prefs** = "compose to my preferences" → **3 plan options** the user picks from (the standard candidate-selection flow).
  - **Surprise Me** = "I don't care, just give me something" → **ONE plan**, taken **directly to that DRAFT plan screen** (skip the 3-candidate picker), with a **"Surprise Me again"** button present **until the user saves** (regenerate-in-place). It's the low-friction "just decide for me" path.
- **Current behavior (wrong):** Surprise Me produces the 3-candidate flow like Use My Prefs — no single-plan-to-draft, no "surprise me again."
- **NOT in Plan-Gen-Arc scope** (Hans: "we don't have to do that in this arc"). Logged so it isn't lost — it was hit in the WS9 3c arc before the gear-switch to the plan-gen arc.
- **Interaction note:** relates to BUG-036 (the surprise-me no-input guard, already FIXED `a658fb6`) and D-WS9-032's 7-point plan-options model (Block 4). Whoever picks this up should reconcile the Surprise-Me single-draft flow with that model.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-036 (surprise-me guard, FIXED) · D-WS9-032 (plan-options flow model) · D-WS9-033 §4 Block 4 (wizard-entry rebuild).

### BUG-040 — Ingredient-`unit` 422 on plan activation ("draft malformed"): curated catalog meals with empty `unit` fail the pre-finalize schema ✅ FIXED (Block 2 headline fix)

- **Surface:** `POST /wizard/drafts/:id/activate` (pre-finalize validation) — triggered when a plan composed with curated catalog (store) meals is activated/saved.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** in Plan-Gen Arc Block 2 (`5c883e3`, device-verified July 17, 2026).
- **Priority:** P1 (blocked the Block 2 commit).
- **Symptom:** Activation 422'd with `event: "wizard_draft_activate_malformed"`, `reason: "meals.N.dishes.0.ingredients.M.unit, ..."`. Per-ingredient (some pass, some fail), not positional. Fast validation (`responseTime` ~165–241ms — the malformation was real, not a timeout). Failed on mostly-fresh plans too (only needs ONE curated store slot to trip).
- **Root cause (VERIFIED end-to-end, CC diagnostic):** the 12 hand-seeded curated meals carried **20 count-only produce ingredients seeded `unit:""`** (limes, avocado, bay leaves, tomatoes, tortillas, etc.). The activation ingredient schema (`WizardExpandDishIngredientSchema`, `schemas/wizard.ts`) requires `unit: z.string().min(1)` — empty string fails. **Regression-by-exposure:** the empty-unit data AND the unvalidated store-compose seam (`composeStoreMealDetails` copied `di.unit` verbatim; the expand route persisted without re-validating the assembled plan) both **pre-existed** in B-2 — but were rarely hit while compose was timid. **Fix 3** (strengthened `CATALOG_SHELF_SECTION`) made shelf usage aggressive, which started binding curated meals into drafts and exposed the bad data. Saves "worked before" because those plans were all-live (live slots are validated at expand — an empty unit there 502s). Hypothesis A (v5 prompt emits bad units) was **REFUTED**: build-plans v5 emits titles only, no ingredients — it's the *trigger* (more store selection), not the *emitter*.
- **The fix (three parts, all in `5c883e3`):**
  1. **Data backfill** (`scripts/backfill-curated-count-units.ts`, one-shot) — 20 curated count-only ingredients `unit:"" → "each"` (verified DB-wide convention: `"each"` has 599 uses; the same ingredients already use `"each"` on live meals). Now a one-time historical cleanup, no-op on a freshly-seeded DB.
  2. **Seed-source durability fix** (`prisma/seed.ts` `parseAmount`) — the fraction branch (`"1/2"` red onion) AND the simple-count branch (`"12"` tortillas) emitted `unit:""`; both now emit `"each"`. The `:37` fallback was **left `""`** (correctly — it's the non-numeric/qualitative catch-all, e.g. "to taste"; blanket-`"each"`ing would mislabel; also dead for the current seed). **This is the durable fix — a re-seed no longer reintroduces the bug, and Block 3's harness inherits the correct parser.**
  3. **Reject-to-live compose gate** (`storeMealDetails.ts` → `wizardExpansion.ts`) — every assembled store meal's ingredients validated against the **same** schema activation uses (no drift by construction). Any failing ingredient → the store meal is **rejected** and the slot **falls back to live** via the existing D-WS9-037 graceful-degrade branch; rejection logged (`event: "store_meal_rejected_invalid_ingredient"`) + counted (`storeMealsRejected` telemetry, so bad Block-3 seed data falling back is visible, not silent). **No coercion** — bad units are never patched/masked (avoids provenance laundering). Gate is store-slot-only (live is already validated at expand).
- **Consistency proof (VERIFIED):** after the data + source fix, all 23 catalog dinner meals have 0 empty-unit ingredients → the gate is **dormant** for the current catalog (curated meals validate clean, compose as store). The three fixes agree by construction; the gate only fires on genuinely-bad future data. Server suite 1281→1282 (+1 reject test); tsc clean.
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-038 (the bind-directly store-compose path) · D-WS9-037 (the graceful-degrade branch the reject reuses) · D-WS7-201 (verifiable-provenance / no-laundering discipline behind the no-coercion ruling) · D-WS9-040 (Block 3 catalog-quality gate — the *quality* of these meals is a separate, ruled Block 3 concern) · the prompt-update / replace-don't-layer discipline (fixing the emitter, not just the data).

### BUG-039 — Surprise Me 500 ("Kiwi got distracted"): `wizard.surprise.generate` was DB-seeded but never registered in the in-memory prompt registry ✅ FIXED

- **Surface:** Home → **Surprise Me** (the `wizard.surprise.generate` AI call path).
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** in Plan-Gen Arc Block 2 fix pass (`5c883e3`, device-verified July 17, 2026). ⚠️ **Bookkeeping note:** this row is written at Block 2 close — the fix-pass edit that was *supposed* to log it in the prior chat was never persisted to this file (the on-disk log was still at the pre-fix-pass `BUG-039` pointer). The fix itself shipped in the same fix pass; only the log entry lagged.
- **Priority:** P2 (surfaced + fixed within the fix pass).
- **Symptom:** Surprise Me returned a 500 "Kiwi got distracted" error screen.
- **Root cause:** `wizard.surprise.generate` existed in the DB seed but was **not registered in the in-memory `promptRegistry`** (`promptRegistry.ts:43`) — so the lookup threw at call time.
- **The fix:** registered the key in `promptRegistry` + added a real try/catch. Device-verified: Surprise Me no longer 500s, generates cleanly.
- **Distinct from BUG-036** (the surprise-me *no-input guard* regression, separately FIXED `a658fb6`) — same surface, different root cause (guard vs. registry).
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-036 (surprise-me guard, FIXED) · BUG-037 (surprise-me 1-plan-to-draft UX, FIXED same fix pass) · D-WS9-019 (prompt/registry infrastructure).

### BUG-030 — Wizard plan-selection: backing out of Plan Details and re-tapping the SAME plan re-runs the AI and duplicates the plan + meals ✅ FIXED (P1)
- **Surface:** Wizard plan generation → 3-plan selection → Plan Details → back-navigation. (Surfaced in the **Prefs wizard** during the July 10 Cookbook Phase B / WS7-8b combined device test, but **almost certainly pre-existing and long-standing** — Hans's read: "it's probably from a long time ago, not the adjustment to preferences." Treat as a latent bug the prefs work merely exposed, NOT a Phase-B regression.)
- **Symptom (repro, device-verified):**
  1. Run wizard generation → the 3-plan chooser appears.
  2. Tap one of the three plans → **View plan details**.
  3. **Navigate back (swipe-left / back gesture)** → returns to the plans page.
  4. **Tap the SAME plan again** → **the AI runs again**, regenerating the plan from scratch.
  5. Result: a **duplicate plan** and **duplicate meals** are created.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — Plan-Gen Arc Block 1, commit `99d6988`** (July 16, 2026; device-verified all 4 cases, pushed). Phase 0 (July 15) verified the root cause — see below; the prior "unverified lead" is now resolved.
- **Priority:** **P1.** Rationale: (a) it burns a **full Sonnet plan-generation call** on an accidental back-tap — real per-user cost on the highest-traffic path; (b) it **pollutes user data** with duplicate plans + duplicate meals; (c) it sits directly on the §9 launch-critical path (signup → plan → grocery), which is the flow Hans has ranked highest-priority. This is a bad first-run experience, not a cosmetic nit.
- **Expected behavior (Hans, July 10):** Back from Plan Details should return to the **plan-selection page** with the already-generated plans **intact and cached** — re-tapping a plan should **re-open** it, never re-generate it.
- **Root cause (Phase 0 verified July 15, 2026 — replaces the prior UNVERIFIED LEAD):** **mechanism-a, eager-persist-with-no-idempotency.** `POST /wizard/expand` did an unconditional `mealPlanInstance.create`, and activate/save unconditionally materialized, with no content-keyed dedup. Hans's recalled persist-gate DOES exist (`isWizardDraft` boolean is the discriminator — `status:"draft"` is a red herring), but it had no idempotency: re-tapping the same candidate minted a fresh draft each time (`candidate.id` is AI-minted / non-durable, so it couldn't serve as a dedup key). The nav-state re-fire theory (mechanism b) was **REFUTED for current code** — the results screen caches candidates; the generate `useEffect` is keyed on `[input, attempt, tellKiwiPayload]`, not focus/mount. TWO dup vectors existed: (a) back-out/re-tap through clean 201s (this bug); (b) dropped-201 phantom activation (already papered over by `resolveActivatedPlanRouteAfter404`).
- **Fix (`99d6988`):** content-derived idempotency key — SHA-256 of `title` + **sorted** meal titles (`lib/wizardContentHash.ts`), persisted on a new `MealPlanInstance.wizardContentHash` column (`@@index([userId, wizardContentHash])`, additive migration, no backfill). Expand reuses an existing unconsumed draft matching the key; activate/save return the existing materialized plan; a **template dedup-on-write guard** (`wizardActivation.ts`, minimal — no schema key, D-WS7-071 stays open for Phase C) stops duplicate `MealPlanTemplate` mints. **Supersede-on-consume** archives sibling + orphan unconsumed drafts (archive + clear-blob, **never flips `isWizardDraft`** — every reader treats `true` as hidden, so a flip would surface orphans). Both dup vectors closed; the 404-recovery helper kept as belt-and-suspenders.
- **Known future interaction (do not design around this without checking):** Hans notes the persist-gate behavior **"may change when we do the AI batch meal and plan creation strategy."** So whoever fixes this should check whether the batch-creation strategy is imminent — a fix that hardcodes today's persist semantics could be immediately obsoleted. Cross-check the roadmap / caching-pregeneration docs before committing to an architecture.
- **Target:** **Unassigned — needs its own block, likely a fresh chat** (Hans: "a completely new chat is likely the one to pick it up"). This is **wizard/plan-gen surface (PREFS-track territory)**, NOT WS7-8b grocery. It is **NOT a WS7-8b close blocker.**
- **Context a fresh chat should NOT have to re-derive:** the bug was found while device-testing commit `0b7ab9e` (Cookbook Phase B close + PREFS Block 2+3: discovery/cook-time/sauce prefs + wizard gen). The prefs work itself tested green — this is orthogonal. Relevant PRD sections for the wizard/plan surface: **§5 (wizard flow), §6 (wizard variants), §7 (wizard inputs), §8 (meal detail), §15 (plans)** — verify these section numbers with a `grep -n '^#'` header skim before relying on them (§26.2).

### BUG-031 — Parmesan cheese shows 3.97 oz (unit-merge: volume↔weight conversion + ⅛-ladder skip on the AI merge path) ✅ FIXED
- **Surface:** Grocery consolidation — cross-unit merge of the same canonical ingredient.
- **Symptom (July 10 device test, post-B1):** Parmesan cheese grocery line reads **"3.97 oz"**. Confirmed inputs: **one recipe calls 3 oz**, **another calls ½ cup**. So the consolidator converted ½ cup parmesan → ~0.97 oz (a **volume→weight** conversion) and summed to 3.97 oz.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — Block B2, commit `2798db0`** (July 12, 2026; device-tested green, pushed). **BOTH angles were real** — the §27 discipline of logging two un-presupposed angles rather than one guessed root cause was correct: neither alone explains the bug.
- **Priority:** was P2.
- **Resolution — the line now reads `1 wedge (6 oz) parmesan (4⅞ oz)`.** Merge math: 3 oz + ½ cup → deterministic **density** merge (85.05 g + 50 g = 135.05 g ÷ 28.35) = **4.7638 oz raw** → **single** ladder sweep → **4.875 oz (4⅞)**.
- **BOTH ANGLES CONFIRMED (each was a genuine, independent defect):**
  - **Angle A — the ⅛-ladder skip was REAL (a B1-coverage gap).** Verified in code at B2 Phase 0, not hypothesized: the `roundNeedQuantity` sweep ran **only** inside `consolidatePlanIngredients` (`groceryList.ts:578-580`), and the route pipeline is `consolidate → fillPurchaseSizes → generateFinalGroceryList` — **nothing re-applied the sweep after the AI ran.** Sonnet's returned items were placed **verbatim** (`groceryListAI.ts:419-421`), so **AI-merged quantities bypassed the ladder entirely** and reached the user as raw off-ladder floats. **Fixed** by re-applying `roundNeedQuantity` to each AI-returned item **after** the merge.
  - **Angle B — the density conversion was an unfounded AI guess.** Confirmed: the ½-cup→oz conversion happened inside the Sonnet rule-2 merge with **zero density reference data** — the model was left to invent a factor for a conversion that is genuinely density-dependent (grated vs shredded vs shaved parmesan differ substantially). **Fixed** by the B2 conversion table; parmesan resolved to **grated, 100 g/cup**, consistent across the curated and `usda_derived` tiers (it was B2's design test case).
- **⚠️ THE LOAD-BEARING ORDERING LESSON — `merge-then-round-ONCE`.** Once density conversion exists, the merge **must** convert to a single unit **first** and ladder-round the **merged total**, never round the parts and then merge (that double-rounds and inflates). CC surfaced this at Phase 0 and it is now **structural, not a discipline**: the density merge sits in the consolidator on **raw** quantities, *before* the single existing round sweep — so merge-then-round-once **falls out of the architecture** and rounding lives in exactly one place. **Test-pinned:** 3 oz + 0.4 cup → 4.5 (round-then-merge) vs **4.875** (merge-then-round) — the test proves they differ.
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-025 (25-1), D-WS7-197, B2 commit `2798db0`. **The AI-merge bypass this bug exposed also protects the table-miss tail** — table-covered ingredients now never reach the AI at all, and the re-sweep snaps anything that does back onto the ladder (unit-pinned: stubbed AI returns 3.97 → output 4).

<details><summary>Original two-angle diagnosis (July 10, pre-B2) — kept for history</summary>

- **TWO ANGLES — deliberately logged as ONE bug, because we don't yet know which (or both) is the real cause. Do NOT presuppose the diagnosis (§27); B2's Phase 0 should determine it:**
  - **Angle A — possible ⅛-ladder SKIP on the AI merge path (B1 residue).** `3.97` is **not on the ⅛ ladder** — B1's `roundNeedQuantity` should have rounded a measured unit UP to an eighth (3.97 → 4). That it didn't suggests this value **reached the user without passing through the sweep**, which points at the **AI merge path (rule 2, unit-mismatch merge)** — exactly the same-canonical/different-unit case this bug IS. Note the rule-2 prompt was fixed in this same session (`0b7ab9e`) to *preserve* the fine-grained need quantity rather than round toward a purchase size — **but "fine-grained" was never meant to mean "off-ladder raw float."** Open question: does the AI-merged quantity get re-run through `roundNeedQuantity` server-side after the AI returns, or does it bypass the sweep entirely? **Verify in code.** If it bypasses, that's a real gap in B1's coverage and the fix is to re-apply the ladder to AI-merged quantities.
  - **Angle B — volume↔weight density conversion accuracy (B2 territory).** Converting **½ cup parmesan → oz** is a **density-dependent** conversion (grated vs shredded vs shaved parmesan differ substantially by volume-to-weight). This is precisely the class of conversion **B2's fuller purchase-size/unit-conversion table (D-WS7-197)** must handle. Whatever number the merge produced, the *conversion factor itself* needs to come from a real density table, not an ad-hoc AI guess.
- **Target:** **Block B2** (cross-ref D-WS7-197, BUG-025-1). B2's Phase 0 should (1) determine whether the AI merge path skips `roundNeedQuantity` (Angle A — if yes, that may be a quick B1-residue fix worth landing separately), and (2) treat parmesan volume↔weight as a **worked example** when designing the conversion table (Angle B).
- **Cross-ref:** BUG-025 (25-1), D-WS7-197 (fuller-table ruling), the rule-2 prompt edit in `0b7ab9e`.

</details>

### BUG-032 — USDA nutrition pointers are wrong-food joins (`sweet potato` → *sweet potato leaves*; `avocado` → *avocado oil*) ✅ FIXED
- **Surface:** `Ingredient` catalog — the `fdcId` inside `nutritionRefPerUnit`. **NOT a grocery bug.** This is the nutrition/macro reference itself.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — commit **`42e99b3`** (`ws7-in-progress`, pushed). Logged July 12, 2026; fixed same day.
- **Priority:** was 🔴 P1 — data-correctness on the launch-critical macro path.
- **Symptom:** Ingredients were matched to USDA food records that are **a different food sharing a token**. USDA's numbers are correct *for the record they describe* — **the join was wrong.**

**⚠️ TWO PREMISES IN THIS ROW'S ORIGINAL TEXT WERE FALSE. Both were disproved at Phase 0 and are corrected here. Do not inherit them.**

- ❌ **"The detector already exists — B2 built it; point `usdaConversionUsable` at all the pointers."** **WRONG.** The guardrail **passes this bug's entire main class, structurally.** Verified live: `sweet potato` → *"Sweet potato leaves, raw"* returns `usable=TRUE`. Every wrong-food row **shares the head noun by definition** (that is what makes it a shared-token join) and carries **no form disqualifier** — so the guardrail cannot see it. B2 caught `avocado`/`black beans` via its hand-curated `DERIVE_DENYLIST`, **not** the guardrail. **Fail is high-precision; pass is uninformative.** A string matcher cannot solve a semantic problem.
- ❌ **"441 pointers, 348 never looked at."** **WRONG, and inverted.** Live counts: **451 total rows / 329 carrying an `fdcId` (the real audit surface) / 122 already-honest miss-markers / zero null.** Every row *had* been processed by the nutrition arc; the untested surface was the 329 accepted by the permissive matcher.
- ❌ **The ~35-bad extrapolation was low by 3×.** Real: **101 of 329 (31%)** — 75 wrong joins + 26 unfetchable. B2's 9-of-93 sample was **biased**: those 93 were rows that *already carried usable portion data*, which skews toward well-structured whole-food records — i.e. the rows *least* likely to be bad. **A sample drawn from "rows that already worked for another purpose" is not a random sample of catalog health.**

- **THE FIX — an Opus judge, not a matcher.** Each of the 329 rows was put to an **Opus** call: *"Does this USDA record describe this ingredient as a recipe would actually use it?"* → `YES` / `NO` / `UNSURE` + reason. The judge saw **three** inputs: `canonicalName`, the FDC `description` (re-fetched — **it is not persisted**), and the **stored `per100g` macros** as an independent cross-check (a "vegetable" at 884 kcal / 100 g fat is not a vegetable — this is what damns `avocado` on sight).
- **THE JUDGE HAD NO WRITE PATH (load-bearing).** A `YES` meant **leave the row untouched** — a non-objection to the status quo, not a new claim. Only `NO`/`UNSURE` rows reached Hans for ruling. **Consequence: the judge could never introduce a wrong value and never upgrade a guess into an authoritative stamp; its worst case was a miss.** The moment a `YES` writes, a hallucinated `YES` launders poison into the canonical catalog wearing a `usda_derived` stamp. **Do not build a `YES` write path.**
- **Known-answer check PASSED** (all five confirmed-bad rows returned `NO`) before the sweep was trusted.
- **Result:** **94 re-matched · 1 stamped miss (`fresh tarragon` — USDA has no fresh-herb record) · 1 accepted no-op (`lime zest`).** Catalog: 329 matched → **328 matched / 123 miss**. api-server **1226 / 0 fail / 2 skip**; tsc clean.
- **Headline fixes:** **`sweet potato`** → the tuber, not the plant's **leaves** · **`avocado`** → **160 kcal flesh**, not *[Oil, avocado]*'s **884 kcal / 100 g fat** (**~5.5× live on the macro path**) · **`egg`** → whole egg (143/9.5/12.6), not a **0-g-fat egg-white** record · `chicken thighs`/`drumsticks` → meat & skin, not *[Chicken, **skin only**]* (440 kcal / 44 g fat — pure skin) · `lean ground beef` → 90/10, not **70/30** (the fattiest grade standing in for the leanest — **inverted**) · grains (`quinoa`/`wild rice`/`rice noodles`) → **dry**, not cooked (a recipe's "1 cup rice" means dry; ~3× mass error) · `yellow potato` → raw potato, not **french-fried frozen**.

- **⚠️ TWO SAVES THAT WOULD HAVE WRECKED THE BLOCK (both caught by CC mid-phase, both ratified):**
  1. **FDC search ranking buries the plain `X, raw` staple** beneath processed/branded variants that repeat the query tokens. `searchFoods("sweet potato")` never surfaces the raw tuber in a rankable window. **Left automated, ~11 rows — including `sweet potato`, the marquee bug of the whole block — would have come back as MISS and been stamped as "honest misses."** The flagship poisoned row would have been **laundered into an honest-looking miss** and the report would have read as a success. Recovered via targeted queries + `getFood` verification, hand-pinned.
  2. **The "13 dead `fdcId`s" were not deleted — they are Foundation Foods.** Foundation records are **searchable but 404 on by-id fetch**; SR Legacy records fetch fine (verified: 8/8 Foundation → 404, 7/7 SR Legacy → OK). → **D-WS7-201.**

- **Cross-ref:** **BUG-033** (`lime zest` portion/density — split out, belongs to B2's `conversionRef` arc, NOT here); **D-WS7-201** (the Foundation-vs-SR-Legacy pointer rule); B2 / BUG-025-1 / D-WS7-197 (exposed it); D-WS6-024 (the USDA arc that laid the pointers); the July-4 "reused, never re-inferred" catalog lesson (why this could never age out — a plan generated tomorrow would still inherit `sweet potato → leaves`).
- **Verification pin (new, permanent):** `ws7-8b-bug032-verify.ts` asserts **zero by-id 404s across all matched pointers** (239 unique `fdcId`s, all fetch) and **no Foundation id anywhere**. This is the regression pin for the class — it is the check that would have caught the whole thing from the start.

### BUG-034 — Pre-v4 palette fork: the crash screen (and the root background) still render in the OLD green design ✅ FIXED
- **Surface:** `artifacts/kiwi/constants/colors.ts` + `hooks/useColors.ts` + `components/ErrorFallback.tsx` / `ErrorBoundary`; separately `app/_layout.tsx:46`.
- **Symptom (two halves, one root cause — stale pre-v4 color sources still live in the tree):**
  1. **The error screen is off-palette.** `constants/colors.ts` is a **live second token source** carrying the superseded **v3-era green scheme** (`primary #3a5235`, `accent #e07c3a`, `background #f4f7f0`, `border #d8e2d0`, `radius 14`). It is consumed by `useColors()` → `ErrorFallback.tsx` → the app-wide crash screen. **So when the app crashes, the user sees the OLD design.** This is a genuine fork, not a re-export of tokens.
  2. **The root navigation background is stale.** `app/_layout.tsx:46` sets the Stack `contentStyle` background to **`#f4f7f0`** (old) rather than v4 paper **`#FBF7EF`**. Every inter-screen navigation flashes the old tint. Highest-value single fix in the Layer 1 sweep.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — **`d1f8d99`** on `ws7-in-progress`, **pushed**. Logged **July 13, 2026** (surfaced by the **WS9 Layer 1 / Block 1 tokens-verify** read-only pass); fixed the same day in **WS9 Layer 2 / Block 2**.
- **The fix:** `ErrorFallback` migrated off `useColors()` → `@/constants/tokens` (and its own offenders cleaned in the same touch: ×3 `borderRadius:8`→`Radius.sm`, `fontWeight` literals→tokens, `rgba(0,0,0,0.5)`→`Palette.background.overlay`, `#000` shadow→`Shadow.card.shadowColor`, **plus `Typography.face.sans[*]` applied to title/message/buttonText/modalTitle — it was the ONE genuine system-font leak in the whole tree**). **`constants/colors.ts` + `hooks/useColors.ts` DELETED.** Root background → `Palette.background.app`. The tracked pre-v4 decoy `attached_assets/design-tokens_1776690602642.ts` deleted. mobile **778 / 0**; tsc clean.
- **⚠️ Layer 1's consumer attribution was TRANSITIVE — CC corrected it (§27).** Layer 1 reported the consumers as *"`ErrorFallback` + `ErrorBoundary`."* **`ErrorBoundary` never imported `useColors`** — it imports the `ErrorFallback` *component* (as its default `FallbackComponent`). The true graph is `colors.ts` ← `useColors.ts` ← **`ErrorFallback` alone**. CC traced it **repo-wide, both directions**, before deleting — which is the only reason the delete was safe. **A file-deletion is exactly where an incomplete consumer list becomes a hard build break.**
- **⚠️ Second correction, self-caught:** the decoy was **tracked in git history**, not untracked — `attached_assets/` is gitignored for *new* files, but that one was already committed, so `git add` staged the deletion normally. CC had initially reported it as "gitignored/never tracked" and **corrected itself in the same report.**
- **Priority:** **P2.** Not commit-blocking, but half of it is *user-visible on the worst possible screen* (a crash), and the other half tints every navigation.
- **Target:** **WS9 Layer 2 (Block 2 — shared components).** Ruled in at Layer 2 commissioning: the fork is a **shared primitive**, so it belongs to the leverage layer even though it isn't a "component." Fix = migrate `ErrorFallback` + `ErrorBoundary` to `@/constants/tokens`, then **delete `constants/colors.ts` and `hooks/useColors.ts`** once no consumer remains; and repoint `_layout.tsx:46` → `Palette.background.app`.
- **⚠️ Decoy on disk (do not confuse):** `attached_assets/design-tokens_1776690602642.ts` is a **stale pre-v4** token file (DM Serif Display, sage-primary, `rgba(20,35,18,…)` overlay). It is **imported nowhere**, but anyone grepping for "the design tokens file" will find it and could mistake it for canonical. **It is NOT `design-tokens_4.ts`.** Delete or rename it in the same touch.
- **Related (NOT part of this bug — tracked separately):** the **12× `rgba(20,35,18,x)` modal scrims** across the six sheets are the same **old-green hue** (v4's overlay is neutral ink). That is one copied pattern, not twelve bugs — Layer 2 absorbs it into a single shared scrim token/component. Called out here because it shares the root cause (pre-v4 color values surviving the v4 migration), but it is Layer 2 *scope*, not a logged defect.
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-021 (FLAG 4 `xxs:10` token amendment) · D-WS9-022 (the orphaned `borderRadius:16` set) — both minted from the same Layer 1 pass.

### BUG-018 — Cook-step sequencing: sides should finish while the main rests ✅ FIXED
- **Surface:** Cooking Sequencer / cook-step ordering quality.
- **Symptom:** Corn was boiled during a 30-min grill and came out well before the meat finished — sides should be sequenced to finish while the main rests, not sit going cold.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `d561f65` (B1) + `141c9b9` (B2), both on `ws7-in-progress`, pushed. Device-verified.
- **Priority:** P2/P3.
- **Root cause:** The Sequencer was a **Sonnet call** (`sequencer.step_ordering`, rate-limited 12/min, one `LLMCallLog` row per Cook Mode launch, uncached) doing **single-resource scheduling with unattended gaps** — arithmetic an LLM is bad at. **The prompt was already correct** (`aiPrompts.ts:1348-1352` said "work backwards from the longest dish," with a worked corn/roast example at `:1391-1409`); the AI had the right instructions, the right data, and still got it wrong. **No prompt edit could fix this.** Captured live on a real 3-dish meal: the AI poured the salsa at minute **10** and portioned sour cream at **11–12** against a serve at **56** — cold accompaniments sitting **44–46 minutes**. It was optimizing **passive-window filling** (PRD §13.5.4 criterion 2) at the cost of **finish-time alignment** (criterion 1) — a *weighting* defect delivered confidently with a plausible-sounding cue. **This was fix-to-conform, not a spec change:** PRD **§13.5.5 [LOCKED]** already states the Sequencer *"runs deterministically on existing step data"* — **and prices it FREE on that basis**; **§13.5.4 [LOCKED]** already makes finish-time alignment its first criterion. The implementation simply did not conform.
- **The fix:** **B1 (`d561f65`)** — restored `isTimingSensitive` (the wizard step contract structurally could not express it: live probe found it **true on 0 of 1959 rows**, a structural sibling of BUG #3 / D-WS7-165), retired `parallelGroup` write-side (**NULL on 1959/1959**; now write-rejected 400, column retained/unwritten, no migration); 5 prompt bodies bumped. **B2 (`141c9b9`)** — new pure `cookingScheduler.ts` + 6 tests; the `sequencer.step_ordering` AI call **deleted** along with `CookingSequenceAIError`, its input schema, the D-WS6-093 superRefine, `sequencerSchema.test.ts`, and 2 obsolete smoke scripts. Single- and multi-dish paths unified. **Proof:** `usedAI=false`, computed in **290ms**, **`LLMCallLog` delta = 0**. PRD §13.5.5's "deterministic / free" claim is now *true*.
- **Invariants established — DO NOT REGRESS:**
  - **Unattended/overlappable = `{preheat, rest, hold}` OR `cook && !isTimingSensitive`.** `isTimingSensitive` is the discriminator; **duration ≠ attention** (a 30-min braise is hands-free; a 3-min sear is not). The predicate in `cookingScheduler.ts` carries a **tamper-proof comment**: narrowing the passive set back to `{rest,preheat,hold}` **silently reintroduces this bug** (Hans's repro is a 30-min grill with `phaseType: cook`).
  - **Serve anchor = longest-total-duration dish, NOT `roleLabel`.** `MealDishLink.roleLabel` is `@default(main)`, so a 3-dish meal with nothing explicitly labeled yields *three* mains. A 40-min side gates the meal exactly as hard as a 40-min entrée.
  - **"Finish every dish at serve" assumes parallelism, but a human serializes active steps.** Under one cook you often **cannot** make all finish times identical. The enforceable invariant is **"nothing finishes materially early / no cold food,"** NOT "all finish times equal."
  - **The 3-minute cost is CORRECT.** Deterministic total was 59 min vs the AI's 56, because it serializes cold assembles instead of overlapping them early. *A scheduler tuned for minimum elapsed time would reproduce the bug.*
  - **No wall-clock ever enters the Sequencer.** Steps carry `startOffsetMinutes` (serve-anchored, ≤0); a consumer supplies T later as `startAt = T − offset`. Keeps DST/timezone bugs (cf. D-WS7-104) out of the scheduler entirely.
  - **`estimatedMinutes` is sound — trust the scalar.** 92.8% of dish-owned steps carry real values; the authoring AI already collapses ranges correctly. **Do NOT build a midpoint/range parser** — there is no stored range to parse. (A chat-Claude assumption CC overturned at Phase 0.)
- **Regressions pinned by name:** **D-WS7-164** (longer roast sequenced first, both land at serve) and **BUG-018** (corn finishes AT serve, not early), plus the attention-lock and no-early-finish invariants.
- **Follow-on:** the seeded `sequencer.step_ordering` prompt row is now **inert (no caller)** but left in place — full retirement needs a stateful reseed + `RETIRED_KEYS` sweep. Logged as **D-WS7-205** (low priority, zero risk — the row cannot be invoked).
- **Cross-ref:** D-WS7-164 (Sequencer ordering quality → server tune).

### BUG-023 — Wizard "pick back up" draft resurfaces after user chooses new results ✅ FIXED
- **Surface:** Kitchen Wizard entry — the "pick back up where you left off?" prompt for unsaved/draft plans.
- **Symptom:** User backs out of the app mid-wizard → next wizard run offers the draft plan (correct) → user declines and generates NEW results → the SAME old draft keeps coming back on subsequent wizard runs. Choosing new results should dismiss/supersede the draft so it stops resurfacing.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — Plan-Gen Arc Block 1, commit `99d6988`** (July 16, 2026; device-verified, pushed). **Root cause (verified):** dismissal was **client-only** — `dismissedDrafts.ts` hid declined drafts via AsyncStorage but the **server row survived**, so the draft reappeared on another device / after a cache clear. **Fix:** new `POST /wizard/drafts/:id/dismiss` archives the owned draft server-side (`isArchived:true` + clears the blob; scoped `id+userId+isWizardDraft:true`, so it can only touch the caller's own unconsumed draft; never flips `isWizardDraft`). Mobile `onDismiss` calls it per visible draft and invalidates the drafts query; AsyncStorage kept as an optimistic hide. The resume list (`isArchived:false`) never offers a dismissed draft again; **undismissed unconsumed drafts still resume** (that feature preserved). Idempotent (missing/consumed id → `{dismissed:false}`, 200). *Also cleared by supersede-on-consume: saving/using a plan now archives sibling drafts, so declining is not the only path that clears them.*
- **Priority:** P3.
- **Target (as-built):** fixed in **Plan-Gen Arc Block 1**, NOT the originally-planned WS9 3c. *(Original target below kept for history.)* Rationale for the move: the arc's Block 1 is exactly the draft-lifecycle rationalization this bug needed, and BUG-023's server-side sweep was explicitly folded into it (the "rides with BUG-030" server-supersede). Fixing it here means the wizard-entry surface is touched once, not twice.
- **Original target:** **WS9 Block 3c** (retargeted July 5, WS9 Session 5 — folds into the D-WS9-014 wizard-entry rework: prefill + entry flow; the fix = choosing new results supersedes/dismisses the draft).
- **Re-reported July 13, 2026** (WS7-8b close) — Hans surfaced it again: *"when a user views a plan, but doesn't save/use and then backs out of the wizard… if the user chooses to get new results the 'pick up where you left off' drafts should be cleared."* **Ruling: do NOT pull into WS7-8b — target unchanged.** It is already correctly logged and correctly targeted; D-WS9-014 is *already rebuilding this exact surface*, and fixing it standalone means touching wizard entry twice.



### BUG-025 — Grocery list quantity/purchase-size math + staples gaps (cluster) ✅ FIXED (25-4 deferred)
- **Surface:** Grocery aggregation / purchase-sizing (server: purchase defaults + list assembly) + staples filtering.
- **Symptom (July 5 device test, screenshots):** "1 head Garlic · 30 clove" (one head ≈ 10–12 cloves — purchase size doesn't cover the need); "2 lemons · 5.5 each" (title/qty contradiction); fractional "each" surfacing raw ("Red onion · 0.75 each", "Yellow onion · 2.5 each"); bananas (Recurring) shows "1 each", should be "1 bunch"; "1 bottle (15 oz) lemon juice · 5 tablespoon" — should be sourceable from lemons already on the list rather than forcing a bottle; **Kosher salt appeared on the list — should be treated as a staple alongside "salt"**.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — **25-2 / 25-3 / 25-5** in **Block B1** (`f74ee29` + amend `605961a`, client sibling `13f48c3`); **25-1** in **Block B2** (`2798db0` + `88b212d` + `b0cd677` + `2f42427`, July 12, 2026, device-tested green). **25-4 remains deferred** (D-WS7-196 — a substitution/derivation engine, not an MVP fix). **The headline symptom is dead:** "1 head Garlic · 30 clove" now reads **"3 heads garlic (30 cloves)"**.
- **Priority:** was P2 (order-blocking quality on the launch-critical grocery path).
- **Target:** WS7-8b **Block B**, executed as the B1/B2 de-risk split. **B1 = 25-2 + 25-3 + 25-5 → DONE.** **B2 = 25-1 → DONE.**
- **Block B2 outcome (July 12, 2026 — four commits):** `2798db0` shared conversion table + `conversionRef` migration + USDA backfill + grocery consumption · `88b212d` macro quantity→grams table swap (**closes D-WS6-024 Step 2**) · `b0cd677` two-part line (pack persisted as data, composed at render) · `2f42427` count-noun need pluralization. api-server **1226 / 2 skip / 0 fail**; mobile **778 / 0**; tsc clean. **PRD §2.8 `[OPEN]` → LOCKED redline applied at this close.**
