### BUG-125 — The grocery row's order line shows the purchase pack verbatim instead of a quantity that covers the need ✅ FIXED

- **Date:** August 21, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list row rendering · **Found by:** Hans, BUG-096 device test items 2, 6 and 7.
- **Symptom, three rows from one list:** `roma tomatoes` shows an order line of **`4 roma tomatoes`** against a need of **9 each**. `garlic` shows **`1 head garlic`** against **6 cloves** — correct. `ear of corn` shows **no order line at all**, just the name and `8 each`.
- **Hans's statement of the intended design, verbatim:** *"it should say the quantity to order ahead of the item. and then the text in parentheses below says how much is going to be used for actual cooking."* And: *"ear of corn shows 8 each, but it should show 8 ears of corn and 8 each."* **Garlic is the only one of the three doing this today.**
- ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS (chat-Claude's), and it is what the probe must settle:** the row prints the purchase-pack **display string verbatim** as its order line rather than computing an order quantity that covers the need — which would explain all three symptoms at once (a pack exists and prints as-is; no pack exists and the raw name prints). **If correct the fix is a rounding step, not a data change.** Correct behaviour for `roma tomatoes` at pack 4 / need 9 would be 3 packs.
- ⚠️ **NEITHER ANSWER IS RIGHT FOR `roma tomatoes` EVEN AFTER ROUNDING**, because "4 roma tomatoes" is a computed need masquerading as a package — **BUG-124.** The two bugs compound and are separately fixable: BUG-125 is display logic, BUG-124 is catalog data.
- **ROOT CAUSE, MEASURED (probe, August 21, 2026):** `artifacts/kiwi/lib/format/grocery.ts:110-130`, `composePackName`, composed at render. For `purchaseUnit === "each"` it strips the leading count from `purchaseDisplay` and compares the residue to the name — on match it **prints the pack verbatim** (roma tomatoes: pack 4, need 9 ignored); on mismatch it **drops the pack entirely** (corn: residue `ears` ≠ `ear of corn` → bare name). Non-`each` units print `"{pack} {name}"` — garlic is **correct only by coincidence** (1 head ≈ 10–12 cloves happens to cover 6). **Nothing anywhere scales the pack to the need** — two search shapes returned zero matches for any ceiling arithmetic over `purchaseQuantity`, and `roundNeedQuantity` is explicit that it rounds the need only and *"is NEVER rounded toward a purchasable amount."* All three device symptoms fall out of that one function.
- ⚠️ **HALF THE PROMPT HYPOTHESIS WAS REFUTED: corn is NOT missing pack data.** It carries `"4 ears"`; 53 of 53 rows on that list have a pack. The no-pack-so-bare-name half was wrong; the pack-printed-verbatim half was confirmed.
- **Structural root, shared with BUG-124:** the pack is stored **per-`Ingredient`** — catalog-level and need-independent — while `aiPrompts.ts:1555` intends it sized to the need at write time (*"raise only when the recipe clearly needs more than one package"*). **A catalog constant cannot cover a per-list need.**
- ✅ **RULED (Hans, August 21, 2026): option (a), display-half first.** Scale the pack to cover the need, stop suppressing it on a qualifier mismatch, and **where no pack exists use the need itself as the order quantity.** Target format, from Hans's own words: *"it should say the quantity to order ahead of the item. and then the text in parentheses below says how much is going to be used for actual cooking."* So: "9 roma tomatoes" / "(9 each)" · "8 ears of corn" / "(8 each)" · "1 head of garlic" / "(6 cloves)". **Accepted trade: over-ordering where the pack is bogus, because under-ordering is the worse failure — you cannot cook with four tomatoes when the recipe wants nine.**
- ⚠️ **Direct evidence the current display confuses even its owner:** during the BUG-126 device pass (August 22), Hans read the verbatim `"4 roma tomatoes"` pack line as a stale quantity and briefly reported the reconcile fix as failing — retracted once the line was recognized as this bug. The ruled format, order-quantity-first, removes exactly that ambiguity.
- ⚠️ **THE RULE IS NOT "SCALE THE PACK" — Hans's own worked examples refuted chat-Claude's framing at build time.** Scaling a 4-pack to cover 9 gives **12**; his ruled output is **9**. What the three rows encode: **when the pack unit and the need unit are the same count unit (`each` vs `each`), the pack is meaningless and the order quantity IS the need.** His reasoning, verbatim: *"roma tomatoes don't always and only get sold in minimums of 4 packs. that's popular, but grocery stores have them loose, too."* A pack whose unit is `each` is a contradiction in terms. **When the units differ, the pack is a real container and is used and scaled.** Corn lands on 8 either way and does not discriminate; tomatoes do.
- ⚠️ **THE EGG COUNTEREXAMPLE WAS RAISED BY HANS AND MEASURED BEFORE BUILD** — *"you can't buy less than 6 eggs."* If eggs were stored as `each`, the same-unit rule would render `1 egg` and **under-order**. Measured: eggs are stored as `dozen`, so the container branch handles them and the rule never fires. **All 32 catalog rows with `each` + quantity > 1 are computed recipe needs, not retail minimums** — the tell is that the numbers move per plan (catalog says 4 roma tomatoes; live rows carry 7).
- **Spec status: code-is-wrong against a LOCKED spec, no redline owed.** The PRD's grocery section already specified the two-part `purchase size (need quantity)` line with the purchase size as the covers-the-need number; that section was redlined to LOCKED July 12, 2026.
- **Ruled and built:** no connector (`"1 head garlic"`, matching the locked examples, not `"of"`) · a **leading-number guard** so the 204 legacy rows whose names already begin with a quantity get nothing prepended (→ BUG-136) · count-unit order quantities ceil (2.5 → order 3, need still shows 2½) · the residue-elide kept as a presentation step decoupled from the quantity decision · **the ~3 rows whose pack names a different food get LOUDER rather than being softened**, as ruled.
- **Device-confirmed twice.** `083d935`: 5/5 blocking items, 284 lines changed, 223 rows gained an order quantity, 56 stopped under-ordering. Two follow-on defects found by that pass (→ BUG-130, BUG-131) plus the need-ceil (→ BUG-132), all fixed in `1c61e8e`, device-confirmed 12/12.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `083d935` + `1c61e8e`, pushed August 22 and 24, 2026 · **Priority:** was P2 · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · BUG-124 · BUG-126 · BUG-130 · BUG-131 · BUG-132 · BUG-136 · D-WS9-179.

### BUG-126 — An ingredient added to a dish never reaches the grocery list, under either save mode ✅ FIXED

- **Date:** August 21, 2026 · **Surface:** add-ingredient-to-dish write path; grocery list generation and reconcile · **Found by:** Hans, BUG-096 device test.
- **Symptom, verbatim:** *"I saved it for this time only and saved it always and neither one got the additional '5 each roma tomato' ingredient on the grocery list. I generated the grocery list again from the plan and it loaded for a while and then gave the toast that the list was updated to match the plan."* **Both save modes failed. An explicit regenerate did not fix it, and reported success.**
- ⚠️ **A TOAST CLAIMING THE LIST WAS UPDATED WHILE NOTHING WAS WRITTEN IS ITS OWN DEFECT SHAPE** — the reconcile path ran and believed it succeeded.
- ⚠️ **CANDIDATE CAUSE FROM THE SAME DAY'S WORK, NOT YET RULED IN OR OUT.** Hans typed **"roma tomato" — singular** — which as of that morning is a **deleted BUG-096 merge loser surviving only as an alias.** BUG-096 made five name→id paths alias-aware (`ingredientResolve`, `groceryList.ts:234`, `groceryReconcile.ts:459`, `groceryLists.ts:136`, `mealCreate.ts:130`). **If the add-ingredient write path is a sixth path, this is a regression introduced by BUG-096 on an unpushed branch.** It may equally be entirely pre-existing.
- **Corroborating detail:** the consolidated `roma tomatoes` row reads **9 each**, consistent with the two pre-existing dish rows (6 + 3) summing and Hans's +5 never landing.
- **What the probe must return:** whether either write landed at all (a `DishIngredient` row at 5 each, or a fourth non-null `recipeOverrideJson` — Phase 0 measured exactly 3 in the whole database); **the full enumeration of name→id resolution sites from scratch rather than membership-checked against the known five**; what the write path does on a resolution miss, since **a silent drop is the shape that matches the symptom**; and a **straight verdict — merge-caused, pre-existing, or undetermined, with no softening of undetermined into probable.**
- **Prior art worth checking:** the BUG-121 probe recorded `groceryList.ts:184-187` stating `ingredientOverrides` is *intentionally* not read, while `recipeOverrideJson` at `:330`/`:354` replaces a position's ingredients wholesale. **If "save for this time only" writes the field that is intentionally not read, that is the whole bug and it is pre-existing.**
- **PROBE VERDICT (August 21, 2026): pre-existing, merge-EXPOSED. Not a lost write.** Both saves landed: a `DishIngredient` row on forked dish `527fa9cb` at **7 each** → `ingredientId d74cded9` (the live merge survivor — so the alias path worked and the candidate cause above is ruled OUT), and `recipeOverrideJson` went 3 → 4 non-null, storing `{"name":"Roma tomato","unit":"each","quantity":7}`. ⚠️ **Hans said "5 each"; the data says 7** — two edits, the second overwrote the first. Doesn't change the diagnosis.
- **The consolidator is correct**, replayed live: `"roma tomatoes" qty=8 each, srcDishes=9e873e48,527fa9cb`, 13 of 13 buckets. The persisted list holds 1 each. Stored signature matches live signature (`fa657b2d…`), proving reconcile saw the identical set and still wrote 12.
- **ROOT CAUSE — `groceryReconcile.ts:273-317`, step 7.** `signaturesMatch` iterates `r.sources` — the row's **own** sources — and never checks whether the live line **gained** a source the row lacks. `unchanged` keys on `mealId` alone, so a forked meal whose dishes were replaced still counts unchanged. `carry = true`, the 8-each line is discarded, step 8 excludes its key from the AI subset. **The 12 rows that did update had stored sources referencing the deleted dish id → `signaturesMatch = false` → re-resolved. The one added ingredient is the only one with no prior source from that meal.**
- **Why merge-exposed rather than merge-caused (WS7-7-A Block 4/5, D-WS7-124 / D-WS7-137):** pre-merge, "Roma tomato" produced a **different bucket key**, so it would have landed as a separate row — the duplicate was accidentally masking the defect. CC declined to soften this to "probably"; the bucket keys are measured, only the counterfactual is reasoned.
- **The regenerate never ran.** One `generate_grocery` event, at 22:42. The route 409s when a non-archived list exists (→ **BUG-129**). The success toast was reconcile-on-read.
- **Fix built in `5db6ee6` (local, unpushed):** `noNewSources` added to the carry test in `groceryReconcile.ts` — a bucket that GAINS a source now re-resolves. Break proof: the guard test was written first and run RED against unmodified production code, reproducing the live symptom exactly (stale row carried instead of re-resolving), with the mutation grepped from the file per §27.5; it also asserts a second untouched row still carries, so an over-firing check would fail it. **Live read-only replay across all 28 plan-linked lists: +8 re-resolutions total (+0.58pp on the AI subset — no cost regression), and all 8 are genuinely stale buckets that would have stayed stale forever, including this exact case** (list `45496d0e`, live qty 8, gained dish `527fa9cb`). Five more lists self-correct on next read (roma tomatoes ×4, apple cider vinegar, mayonnaise, Parmesan).
- **Device-confirmed (August 22, 2026, 4/4 blocking items):** repeated dish edits propagated to the list on next open with correct consolidation math every time (7→5→other values, always including the second meal's 1 each); both save modes landed a fresh add; a full wizard generation ran clean; and the cold-read discriminator on old list `7d1d049c` rendered the corrected **11 each on the very first read** — the server composes the reconciled result in-response, as designed. ⚠️ **An apparent first-open miss on item 1 was RETRACTED by Hans** — he had read BUG-125's verbatim `"4 roma tomatoes"` pack line as a stale quantity. No residual defect; nothing was minted on the withdrawn observation (§27).
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `5db6ee6`, pushed August 22, 2026 in `b182e84..4fa9a37`, device-confirmed · **Priority:** was P1 (the *"I just did that"* class under D-WS9-174) · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · BUG-121 · BUG-125 · BUG-128 · BUG-129 · D-WS7-124 · D-WS7-137 · D-WS9-174.

### BUG-128 — `wizardExpansion.ts:281` is a sixth name→id path and was left alias-blind by BUG-096 ✅ FIXED

- **Date:** August 21, 2026 · **Surface:** wizard macro-grounding lookup, api-server · **Found by:** CC, disclosed against itself during the BUG-126 read-only probe.
- **Mechanism (CC's finding, verbatim from the probe report):** the D-WS9-050 macro-grounding lookup does `prisma.ingredient.findMany({ where: { canonicalName: { in: [...wantedCanon] } } })` keyed by `ingredientCanonicalKey(ing.name)`, with no alias fallback. BUG-096's Phase 0 enumerated five name→id sites and made all five alias-aware; this one was missed because it keys by `canonicalName` in a batch `in` clause rather than through any of the audited helpers.
- **Effect:** post-merge, every wizard-generated mention of one of the 81 merged-away names (`roma tomato`, `garlic clove`, `bay leaf`, …) misses the catalog, so `toEffectiveIngredient` receives no row and the per-dish macro estimate runs ungrounded — no `nutritionRefPer100g`, no `conversionRef`. **It degrades silently:** the estimate still returns, `macroGroundedPct` is stamped lower, and nothing errors.
- ⚠️ **CORRECTION TO CC's REPORT: this is a live regression on PUSHED code.** The report said "unpushed branch `ws7-in-progress`"; the push (`ffb6603..b182e84`) landed while the probe was running. Merge-caused, introduced 2026-08-21.
- **Fix — the prescribed mechanism was REFUTED at build (CC, same day):** the one-call routing through `lookupIngredientsByName` does not work — `ingredientLookup.ts:143-146` selects `{ id, canonicalName }` only and returns `IngredientNameMatch = { id, canonicalName, matchedVia }`, while `toEffectiveIngredient` reads `row.nutritionRefPerUnit` and `row.conversionRef`. **One-call routing would have traded a silent name miss for a silent column miss** — same ungrounded estimate, harder to spot because the lookup now "succeeds." The keying half of the prescription was right; the diagnosis was sound; only the mechanism was wrong. **Implemented as a two-step:** resolve name→id through the shared alias-aware helper, then fetch the grounding columns by resolved id — two batched queries, `rowByCanon` still keyed by the caller's `primaryKey`, call site unchanged. Built in **`4fa9a37` (local, unpushed)**. Guard: red-proven with the alias hop disabled (no-op check grepped from the file per §27.5), asserting on **what reaches the estimator, not the macros it returns** — the estimator is stubbed, so an output assertion would have been tautological. Restore hash-verified. **Blast radius measured: no backfill needed** — the merge landed 2026-08-21 14:08 and the newest wizard draft predates it; ceiling 46 post-merge dishes / 33 grounding-stamped; the sole surviving `wizardDraftPayload` is all store-sourced slots, which never enter the buggy path. Forward exposure is real (14.4% of `DishIngredient` rows point at fold survivors), which is why the forward fix matters.
- ⚠️ **The find itself is a §27.3 escalation:** both of CC's differently-shaped searches missed this site, because `lookupIngredientByName` builds its `canonicalName` value from a ternary — no value-shaped search can match a computed value. Found only by reading the file. A §27.6 amendment candidate is queued for WS9 close (two shapes agreeing ≠ two shapes being complete).
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `4fa9a37`, pushed August 22, 2026 in `b182e84..4fa9a37`; guarded by two suite tests (merged-away name grounds through the alias; unknown name still sent ungrounded, never dropped) and device-confirmed via a clean end-to-end wizard generation · **Priority:** was P2 · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · BUG-122 · BUG-126 · D-WS9-050 · D-WS9-176.

### BUG-130 — The order line rendered a plural noun at a count of 1 ("1 lemons") ✅ FIXED

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list detail · **Found by:** Hans, device test of `083d935`. **Introduced by `083d935` itself** — a same-day regression from the BUG-125 fix.
- **Mechanism:** the rule-1 branch swaps the computed count in front of `purchaseDisplay`'s stored residue, and that residue is authored plural (`"2 lemons"` → residue `lemons`). At `q === 1` nothing singularises it. The other two shapes (`pluralizeIngredientName`, no-pack) are no-ops at `q ≤ 1` and were correct.
- **Live population: 23 rows**, all on the residue-swap path — Lemon ×9, jalapeño ×5, Lime ×3, english cucumber ×3, plus three bell-pepper/tomato rows.
- ⚠️ **WHY 25 GREEN GUARDS MISSED IT: every `each` call in `grocery-format.test.ts` used a count of 9, 5, 2, 8, 3, 6, 4 or 12. The minimum was 2. No test ever entered rule 1 at 1.** This is now a standing guard requirement — every quantity fix carries a count-of-exactly-1 case.
- **Fix:** at `q === 1`, prefer the ingredient name over the stored residue. Clears 22 of 23 with no singulariser. **`roma tomatoes` is the one row with a plural name and no singular fallback — ruled to stay plural rather than build a stemmer for one row.**
- ⚠️ **A guard for this fix stayed GREEN under mutation and was rewritten** (§27.4): the branch carried a `&& !isPluralWord(lastWord(name))` condition that could never change an output, because that branch only runs when the residue already names the item. Dead code deleted, mutation retargeted at what the guard actually pins.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `1c61e8e`, pushed August 24, 2026 in `083d935..1c61e8e`, device-confirmed · **Priority:** was P2 · **Cross-ref:** BUG-125 · BUG-131 · BUG-132.

### BUG-131 — No pack scaled to the need outside the single garlic case; measure-unit rows under-ordered ✅ FIXED

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list detail · **Found by:** Hans, device test of `083d935` — *"the air fryer crispy beef sliders calls for 1 3/4 lbs ground beef, and the grocery list says 1 lb ground beef."* Second symptom: `1 bunch fresh cilantro (2 bunches)`.
- ⚠️ **This was UNDER-ORDERING — the failure mode ruled worst — and it was pre-existing, not introduced by `083d935`.**
- **Mechanism, measured:** `scalePurchaseForSubUnit` (`ingredientConversions.ts:400`) requires `conv.subUnit`, and **exactly 1 of 1,570 catalog rows has one — garlic.** 236 rows carry a `conversionRef`; 203 have `gramsPerCup`, 57 `gramsPerEach`, **1 has `subUnit`.** Every other non-`each` pack returned `null` on line one and printed verbatim regardless of need. **Garlic was never the representative case; it was the only case**, and it is why the container branch was assumed correct in BUG-125.
- **Measured under-ordering across 1,774 live rows:** 36 rows where need unit == pack unit (pure arithmetic, no data needed) · 8 rows resolvable by parsing the size hint already present in `purchaseDisplay` (`"1 can (14.5 oz)"`) · **1,022 rows conversion-blind and NOT defects** — dominated by tsp-need against container-pack, where one container obviously covers one teaspoon. ⚠️ **Do not read 1,022 as a defect count.**
- **Fix — client-side in `composePackName`, ratified against the same test that decided BUG-125's split:** the three inputs (`purchaseQuantity`, `purchaseUnit`, need) are already on the wire, `conversionRef` is not needed, and the server's PATCH still does not recompute the pack — a server-side fix would rebuild exactly the staleness BUG-125 moved client-side to avoid. Same-unit arithmetic plus a size-hint parse kept as a pure function of `purchaseDisplay`.
- **A `UNIT_ALIASES` map was required for the fix to fire at all** — the need side says `pound`/`ounce` where the pack side says `lb`/`oz`; without it the same-unit branch never matched.
- ⚠️ **A guard stayed GREEN under mutation and was rewritten** (§27.4): the size-hint guard used a 3-cup need against a 14.5-oz hint, where `ceil(3/14.5)` and no-scaling both render as one pack — structurally incapable of failing. Retargeted to a 30-cup need; both cases now asserted.
- **Verified before/after on live rows:** Ground beef `1 lb` → **`2 lb`** · fresh cilantro `1 bunch` → **`2 bunches`** · crushed tomatoes `1 can (14.5 oz)` → **`4 cans (14.5 oz)`** · garlic unchanged at both 6 and 45 cloves. **161 of 1,774 lines change; 42 order more than before.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `1c61e8e`, pushed August 24, 2026, device-confirmed (Hans: *"it did exactly the 4 cans = 56 oz of san marzano tomatoes"*) · **Priority:** was P1 on the under-ordering rule · **Cross-ref:** BUG-124 · BUG-125 · BUG-130 · BUG-132.

### BUG-132 — The count-unit need was ceiled to a whole number, hiding real changes and violating the locked spec ✅ FIXED

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** consolidation / grocery need · **Found by:** the BUG-125 findings probe, while diagnosing two symptoms Hans reported as unrelated.
- **Mechanism:** `roundNeedQuantity` applied `Math.ceil` to any unit absent from `MEASURED_UNITS` — `each`, `clove`, `bunch` among them. Two production call sites, `groceryList.ts:550` and `groceryListAI.ts:520`, both on the generate path, which reconcile reaches through `consolidatePlanIngredients`.
- ⚠️ **IT EXPLAINS BOTH OF HANS'S "MISSING" SYMPTOMS, AND BOTH OF CHAT-CLAUDE'S HYPOTHESES WERE WRONG.** The ½ lemon *did* reach the list — the bucket gained the source and `noNewSources` fired correctly — but `0.5` and `0.5 + 0.5` both ceil to `1`, so a real change displayed identically. And the cilantro `2 bunches` was not fraction-rounding-before-summing (refuted; `groceryList.ts:443` sums raw and `:550` rounds once, after the merge) — it was a **third source Hans did not know about**, a 0.25-bunch contribution from the charred street corn: `0.5 + 0.5 + 0.25 = 1.25`, ceiled to 2.
- **Spec violation:** the locked grocery section states the need *"must stay fine-grained and must NEVER be rounded toward a purchasable amount."* `1.25 bunch → 2 bunch` is exactly that. `083d935` had also made the order line responsible for rounding up, so the need-side ceil was redundant as well as wrong.
- ⚠️ **A CONTRADICTION INSIDE TWO CC REPORTS GATED THIS FIX AND RESOLVED CLEANLY.** `083d935`'s device test showed a live `3 Yellow onions (2½ each)` — impossible if `each` is always ceiled. Verdict: applied at write time, and that row predates it. **Only 8 live rows hold a fractional count need and all 8 sit in the three oldest lists (July 5, 8, 10); `roundNeedQuantity` shipped July 12 in `2798db0`, and every list since holds zero.** The corpus was the decisive evidence, not the code read.
- **Fix:** ceil removed from the need, retained on the order line. `MEASURED_UNITS` became dead and was deleted. **No backfill written** — 604 live rows hold whole-number count needs and self-heal when reconcile re-derives. Four tests retitled, each stating what it used to claim.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED** — `1c61e8e`, pushed August 24, 2026, device-confirmed on a freshly generated plan (Hans: *"i added a 1/2 lemon to a random meal in a plan and it worked as expected"*) · **Priority:** was P2 · **Cross-ref:** BUG-125 · BUG-126 · BUG-131.

### BUG-134 — Same ingredient, same unit, two rows on one list (8 groups)

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list · **Found by:** the BUG-125 findings probe (Root E). Corroborated on device by Hans seeing undeduped lime rows.
- **Shape:** ⚠️ **the consolidation bucket key is `` `${normalizeIngredientName(canonicalName)}|${unit}` `` — so same-ingredient, same-unit duplicates are supposed to be IMPOSSIBLE.** They exist anyway: `garlic cloves` at 10 each beside `garlic cloves` at 2 each · `roma tomatoes` at 3 beside `roma tomatoes` at 6 · `Lime` at 7 beside `Lime` at 2. **Both rows carry sources and different `purchaseDisplay` values, i.e. they were generated at different times against different catalog state.**
- **Scale:** 11 of 32 lists carry ≥2 rows on the same `ingredientId`; 20 duplicate groups, of which **8 are same-unit (this bug)** and 12 are unit-splits (working as designed — cups and bunches cannot sum without a conversion).
- **Candidate mechanism, UNPROVEN and explicitly not claimed:** delta-scoped reconcile inserting a fresh row for a bucket that already had an untouched one. **One group has a different, known cause** — a user-added `lemon` sitting beside a generated `2 lemons` (`isUserAdded=true, sources=0`): the add path does not merge into an existing bucket.
- ⚠️ **SEQUENCING CONSTRAINT, LOAD-BEARING: probe this BEFORE any catalog fold (BUG-137).** Merging ingredient ids turns some of these same-unit duplicates into same-id duplicates and destroys the evidence.
- ✅ **ROOT CAUSE PROVEN (catalog probe, August 24, 2026) — and it is the BUG-096 MERGE, not reconcile.** ⚠️ **CC's own delta-reconcile candidate was REFUTED by its own probe.** The mechanism is `ws9-bug096-ingredient-merge.ts:722-724`: `groceryListItem.updateMany({ where: { ingredientId: loser.id }, data: { ingredientId: survivor.id } })` — **a repoint with no bucket check.** Any list already carrying both a survivor-side row and a loser-side row in the same unit became a same-id/same-unit duplicate the instant the merge ran. Reconcile never touched them.
- **Three independent lines of evidence, any one of which would carry it:**
  1. **The pack strings are the merge CSV's own survivor/loser packs**, verbatim from `scripts/output/bug096-pack-20260821-1408.csv` — e.g. `garlic cloves` survivor `"13 cloves (1–2 bulbs)"` beside loser `"1 head of garlic"`; `roma tomatoes` survivor `"4 roma tomatoes"` beside loser `"7 roma tomatoes"`. **All 7 generated pairs are one row wearing the survivor pack beside one wearing the loser pack.**
  2. **Physical row location.** `grocery_list_items` has no `createdAt`, so CC dated the writes by `ctid`. An UPDATE relocates the tuple: every loser-pack row sits on pages 42–43 while its twin sits on pages 19–35. **The table spans pages 0–74, so 42–43 is a band, not the append tail — one bulk update event.**
  3. **Incidence.** Lists created ≤ 2026-08-21 14:00: **26 lists, 8 with duplicates.** Created after: **13 lists, 0 with duplicates.** Six lists generated August 24 have none.
- **Why reconcile is exonerated:** step 7 keys carries as `id:<uuid>|<unit>` and step 8 filters the resolution subset on the same id-based key, so a carried bucket is always excluded from re-resolution; and the consolidator buckets off one `Ingredient` row, so it cannot emit two lines for one `ingredientId`+unit. **Neither step can produce this shape.**
- ⚠️ **THE FORWARD HAZARD IS THE WHOLE POINT: this defect is still in the merge script, and BUG-137's fold will reproduce it at larger scale.** Folding `garlic cloves` (26 live rows) into `garlic` (32 live rows) mints fresh duplicates in every list carrying both, by that exact `updateMany`. **A duplicate-bucket guard is a PREREQUISITE for any further fold, not a follow-up.**
- **The lemon group is a different mechanism, and the add path is quoted.** `groceryLists.ts:763-782` is a bare `prisma.groceryListItem.create` — **no `findFirst` on `(groceryListId, ingredientId, unit)`, no merge, no warning.** It resolves an `ingredientId` alias-aware and then writes a second row into a bucket that may already exist. Arguably deliberate (user Extras are a lane reconcile never touches) — **so this is a ruling before it is a fix.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED `8c234f0` — DEVICE-VERIFIED August 24, 2026 (Hans, 9/9 in-scope items pass)** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-096 · BUG-125 · BUG-133 · BUG-137 · BUG-140 · BUG-141 · D-WS7-124 · D-WS7-218 · D-WS7-219 · D-WS9-183 · D-WS9-184 · D-WS9-185.

**THE FIX (`8c234f0`).** `planGroceryBucketMerges`, a **pure function in `src/lib/ingredientMergeCarriers.ts`** — ⚠️ **deliberately in `src/` and NOT in the script, which is CC's own call and the reason the deliberate breaks were possible at all: `scripts/` sits outside tsconfig `include` (D-WS7-218), so logic living there cannot be unit-tested.** The script now plans outside the transaction like every other carrier, and the repoint is scoped `id: { in: repointIds }`, so absorbed rows are drained and deleted rather than moved onto the survivor id. **Merge semantics: SUM quantities · UNION sources · delete the emptied row.**

**Backfill applied to all 7 generated groups**, archived and active alike (there is no real customer data, so preservation is never the constraint): garlic cloves 2+10→12, 1+19→20, 1+19→20 · lime 7+2→9 ×2 · roma tomatoes 4+1→5 and 3+6→9. **Verified after the fact: 0 generated duplicate buckets · 0 absorbed rows alive · 0 sources dropped · 0 orphans · 2067→2060 live rows.** Every figure reconciles exactly against the probe's pre-merge row table.

⚠️ **THE USER-ADDED `lemon` GROUP WAS DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED, AND THAT IS NOW A PRODUCT RULING.** Hans, verbatim: *"it's grocery shopping for meals, and the extras and recurring are separate and up to the user, and deliberately added by the user."* **So `(groceryListId, ingredientId, unit)` is legitimately NON-UNIQUE in the domain** — see D-WS9-183, where that ruling is what kills the database constraint. Device-confirmed: the generated `2 lemons` and the user-added `lemon` in Extras both survive, separate, in their correct sections.

**THREE DISCLOSED DEVIATIONS FROM THE COMMISSIONED SPEC, all CC's calls, all accepted:**

1. ⚠️ **The keep-row rule is "the row whose pack matches the catalog," NOT "the survivor-side row" as chat-Claude ruled — and CC was right twice over.** The instruction was **unexecutable**: post-merge both rows already point at the survivor, so survivor-side is not readable from the data. It was also **wrong**: for `garlic cloves` the BUG-096 pack sheet's decision was LOSER, so survivor-side would have installed `13 cloves (1–2 bulbs)` — **a pack Hans explicitly rejected in review.** The choice is written to the sheet as `keep_row_id` and read back on apply, so any row is overridable by editing one cell. **The rule diverged from the commissioned one only on garlic; lime and roma tomatoes were identical either way.**
2. **Source UNION drops a `(mealId, dishId)` the keeper already holds, and counts it.** The literal ruling ("preserve every source reference") would duplicate a source triple and corrupt reconcile's `noNewSources` and signature checks. CC followed the `PrepStepCompletion` precedent already in that script (collision → delete, both counts reported). ⚠️ **Zero collisions occurred live, so this is UNTESTED in either direction. If a dish can legitimately reference one ingredient twice, the dedup drops a real row.** Low risk, recorded rather than chased.
3. **Soft-deleted rows are exempt alongside user-added, and exempt rows do NOT occupy a bucket.** Not in the spec. Merging a soft-deleted row away destroys a restore target (D-WS6-082 requires restore reuse the same row id); merging into one resurrects deleted need. ⚠️ **The second half is the non-obvious one and it is the trap that was named in advance: without it, an exempt row would silently DEFLECT a genuine collision and the duplicate would survive, green.** 0 soft-deleted rows exist, so this is unit-tested only.

⚠️ **THE GUARD IS PINNED BY FOUR DELIBERATE BREAKS, AND BREAK B IS WHY THE SET MATTERS.** Baseline hash `d61e28c9…`, every restore `cmp`- and hash-verified back to it, `grep -c "BREAK-"` = 0 at the end. **A: remove the bucket check → 5/10 red. B: SUM→max → 5/10 red, and it produces the CORRECT ROW COUNT with a WRONG QUANTITY — a signature Break A cannot distinguish, so without it the SUM semantics would have shipped unpinned. C: drop the user-added exemption → 1/10 red. D: let exempt rows occupy a bucket → 2/10 red.** Break E watched the new post-apply assertion go red **against the live database** (6 of 7 merged, 1 duplicate left standing, FAIL printed with the offending group), then restored and re-ran, which also proved idempotence — the quantity write is absolute, not relative, so a re-apply cannot double a keeper.

⚠️ **CC WALKED INTO THE PIPE TRAP AND CAUGHT ITSELF.** Break E first reported `EXIT=0` **because it was piped to `tail`** — precisely the §6 failure the prompt names. Re-run redirected to a file: real exit 0. **Self-disclosed.** It also found and fixed a P2025 it had introduced: `gliNameRewrite` runs later in the same transaction and would have thrown on a row the merge had just deleted.

**Also landed in this commit:** the post-apply duplicate-bucket assertion (D-WS9-183's replacement for the impossible database constraint), scoped to `isUserAdded = false AND deletedAt IS NULL`, printing an explicit PASS rather than a silent one, and aborting **before** the loser delete so a failure leaves nothing orphaned. Plus **D-WS7-219 honoured** (`scripts/output/*` + `!scripts/output/*.csv` — ⚠️ **CC corrected the form: git does not descend into an excluded directory, so a negation nested under `scripts/output/` could never fire**) and **D-WS7-218 honoured** (both scripts typechecked individually, unpiped; `"scripts"` NOT added to `include`). Suite 1651 / 0 fail / 2 pre-existing skips.

⚠️ **TWO RESIDUALS CARRIED FORWARD, NOT RESOLVED HERE:**

- **The merge script's own plan→tx wiring has NEVER EXECUTED** — all 81 fold pairs were already merged, so the script returns at "Nothing to do" before reaching the new code. **Its first real run is BUG-137's garlic fold, and the dry-run cannot preview it.** Full detail and the ruled mitigation: **D-WS9-185**.
- ✅ **THE ONE-UNIT DISCREPANCY ON `c6ff50af` IS CLOSED (Root-B Phase 0, August 25, 2026) — THE SOURCES SUM TO 9, NOT 8.** Row `7240e072`, quantity 9: Pico de Gallo 4 + Slider Toppings 2 + Caprese Chicken 3. **Corroborated independently by a plan-side walk** (`MealPlanItem` → `Meal` → `dishLinks` → `DishIngredient` across all 4 meals = 9), and the pre-merge 3+6 split reconstructs exactly. **The "8" was a mis-recollection; the backfill was correct.** ⚠️ **Two chat-Claude framing errors worth keeping: `GroceryListItemSource` has NO contribution column (`schema.prisma:963-984`; contributions must be reconstructed via `dishId` → `DishIngredient` × `servings/servingsDefault`), and the row being asked about NO LONGER EXISTED — the list full-replaced at 2026-08-25T14:05Z with fresh ids (BUG-143).**
- ~~Superseded~~ — the original framing of this residual: The backfill wrote **9** (3+6, corroborated independently by BUG-125's own recorded need for that exact list). Hans's device pass reported editing the need **"to 7 from 8"** — i.e. an observed **8**. He then overrode the row, so the pre-edit value is no longer recoverable from `quantity`. **NOT systematic: `641eb305` matched its predicted 5 exactly.** ⚠️ **The one cheap discriminator that survives: the row's 3 source rows should still sum to 9. Query them at BUG-137's Phase 0 before assuming this was a mis-recollection.**

### BUG-138 — The same-unit rule discards a sub-unit pack, ordering a quantity of something no store sells

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list detail · **Found by:** the catalog probe, from Hans's device observations `12 garlic cloves (12 each)` and `4 lime wedges (4 each)`. ⚠️ **Introduced by BUG-125's Rule 1** — the same-unit rule chat-Claude derived from Hans's ruled examples.
- **Mechanism:** where the need unit and the pack unit are both `each`, Rule 1 treats the pack as meaningless and uses the need as the order quantity (`grocery.ts:421-444`). **That is correct for loose-sold produce — a "pack" of `each` tomatoes is a contradiction in terms — and wrong when the ITEM ITSELF is a sub-unit of something purchasable.** You buy limes, not lime wedges; heads, not cloves; ears, not kernels. **The stored pack (`1 lime`, `1 head of garlic`) was the right answer and is thrown away.**
- **Live population: 45 rows** — `garlic cloves` ×26, `ears of corn`/`ear of corn` ×12, `lime wedges` ×5, plus two singletons.
- ⚠️ **THE RULE ITSELF IS STILL RIGHT; ITS GUARD IS TOO WIDE.** The discriminator is not the unit pair — it is whether the row names a sub-unit of a purchasable parent. **Do not revert Rule 1** (it is what fixed 223 rows and Hans's roma-tomato case). **BUG-137's garlic fold removes 26 of the 45 by giving those rows a real `subUnit` path, so fixing the fold first may shrink this to ~19 before any code is written.**
- ⚠️ **POPULATION CORRECTED TO ~29, NOT 45, AND THE CORN ROWS DO NOT BELONG (Root-B Phase 0, August 25, 2026).** Fresh Rule-1 census: `garlic cloves` **23** (+1 `, peeled`) · `lime wedges` **5** · corn ×~17. **CC argues corn out of scope and the argument holds: an ear of corn is ITSELF purchasable — a "4 ears" pack is a BUNDLE, not a parent.** Rule 1 ordering 8 ears rather than two 4-packs is the same behaviour that is CORRECT for tomatoes. **Corn is a separate class (bundle vs. minimum) and folding it in makes the discriminator unfindable.** Genuine sub-unit rows: **24 + 5 = 29.**
- ⚠️ **`Ingredient.subUnit` DOES NOT EXIST — CANON WAS WRONG, INCLUDING THIS ENTRY'S EARLIER FRAMING.** It is a **key inside `conversionRef Json?`** (`schema.prisma:391-421`), validated at `ingredientConversions.ts:373-382`. DB census: **1,570 ingredients, exactly 1 carries it** — `garlic = {parent:"head", perParent:10}`.
- **Every candidate field was enumerated against live rows and none can carry the distinction:** `defaultUnit` no (`garlic cloves` and `lime wedges` are both `each` — **the sub-unit-ness is in the NAME**) · `purchaseUnit`/`purchaseQuantity` no (the parent survives only as display prose) · `category` no · the row's `unit` no (**that is precisely the field that collapses to `each` and causes the bug**). **Only `conversionRef.subUnit` has the right shape.**
- ✅ **DO NOT AUTHOR `subUnit` DATA BEFORE THE FOLD — THE FOLD FIXES 24 OF 29 FOR FREE.** `garlic cloves` folds into `garlic` (`e6753984`), which **already carries the correct `subUnit`**; re-resolution moves `purchaseUnit` from `each` to `head`, Rule 1 stops firing, rows render `2 heads Garlic`. ⚠️ **Authoring first would be authoring data for rows the fold deletes.** Residual: **`lime wedges` ×5.**
- 🔴 **RE-MEASURED September 8, 2026 (Lane A, read-only) AND CANON'S PREDICTION DID NOT HOLD. Predicted residual ~5 (`lime wedges` only). ACTUAL: 45 — `garlic cloves` 38, `lime wedges` 7.** ⚠️ **469 live rows fire Rule 1 in total, but the overwhelming majority are CORRECT (lime, onion, lemon, roma tomatoes — loose-sold produce). Corn remains excluded and stays excluded.**
- ✅ **SPLIT BY THE FOLD'S SHIP DATE (`beba57d`, September 6): 42 before, 3 after, across 8 post-fold lists.** ⚠️ **Small denominator, stated rather than buried. So the fold LARGELY worked for new lists and did not close this.**
- 🔴 **THE RESIDUAL MECHANISM, AND IT IS A FINDING ABOUT D-WS9-189 RATHER THAN ABOUT RULE 1: A SOLO `garlic cloves` ROW NEVER ENTERS THE FOLD AT ALL.** A list whose only garlic-family row is `garlic cloves` has **one fold-group member and one unit, so rule 3 cannot fire.** It builds on the deterministic path, **which passes no group ladder**, and `resolveConversion("garlic cloves")` finds none by either route — **the curated table keys on `garlic`, and the catalog row's `conversionRef` is `usda_derived` with no `subUnit`.** ✅ **BUG-215 fixed the AI-MERGE landing; a solo row never reaches it.** **Live instance: list `cb5c8f6e` (September 7) — `garlic cloves 16 clove`, `purchaseUnit "each"`, pack `1 head of garlic`.**
- 🔴 **RE-SCOPED: DO NOT WIDEN RULE 1. THE FIX MOVES TO THE SERVER AND IS STRICTLY SMALLER — resolve the ladder from the FOLD-GROUP KEY (`lookupConversion(relations.groupKey(name))`).** ✅ **Measured blast radius: 1 of 1,573 catalog ingredients carries a ladder, so it reaches the garlic family and NOTHING ELSE; 43 live rows qualify, all garlic.** ⚠️ **This moves BUG-138 out of `artifacts/kiwi/lib/format/grocery.ts` and into the server — the client Rule-1 framing above is superseded and is kept only because it explains WHY the row renders wrong.**
- ✅ **THE FIX IS WRITTEN, GUARDED AND BREAK-VERIFIED, AND WAS THEN DELIBERATELY WITHDRAWN** — held at `scratchpad/with-bug138.keep`. **It changes list contents and would have contaminated BUG-217's acceptance run, which was doing double duty as BUG-214's reproducibility measurement.** ✅ **That measurement is now BANKED, so the fix may land in its own commit with its own before/after.**
- 🔴 **SEQUENCING: BUG-138's ladder resolution must land BEFORE OR WITH any change to `conversionForGroup`'s basis — see BUG-217's trap. The basis fix alone converts a safe refusal into a silent under-order.**
- ✅ **FIXED — `bd2590e`, its own commit on top of `5cecc7c`. The withheld diff was restored BYTE-IDENTICALLY (hash `4fb09de4…` matched the pre-withdrawal record); nothing changed on the way back in.** **Suite 1943 → 1945, +2 guards, typecheck clean.** ✅ **The §2 prohibition on touching `conversionForGroup`'s basis was verified MECHANICALLY — a grep over the diff for the basis symbols returns no hits — rather than by eye.**
- ✅ **BROKEN TWO WAYS, BECAUSE ONE BREAK UNDER-TESTED THE CLAIM. CC's own catch, unprompted, and it is the §27.4 lesson advancing.** Break 4a (drop the group ladder from the deterministic path) proves only that *a ladder was passed*; **break 4b (`lookupConversion(name)` for `lookupConversion(key)`) proves it came FROM THE FOLD-GROUP KEY — which is what the commit subject claims.** Both RED on `'each' !== 'head'`; both restored to `4fb09de4…`. ⚠️ **Under 4b the NEGATIVE guard stayed green — a solo row whose fold group has no ladder is left untouched — so the blast radius holds independently of the break.**
- ✅ **LIVE PROOF ON THE NAMED ROW: list `cb5c8f6e` moved from `purchaseUnit "each"` to `purchaseUnit "head"` — the field Rule 1 actually reads, so Rule 1 can no longer discard the pack.** ⚠️ **The full-list A/B is DISPLAY-LEVEL ONLY (one line of 65 changed, `1 head of garlic` → `1 head`); the `each` → `head` attribution rests on the two break tests. CC flagged that substitution rather than letting the A/B imply more than it showed.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `bd2590e`, LOCAL** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Target:** done · **Cross-ref:** BUG-125 · BUG-131 · BUG-133 · BUG-137 · **BUG-215** · **BUG-217** · D-WS9-189.

### BUG-140 — Every in-list "+ add item" control is a dead no-op

- **Date:** August 24, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list, mobile (`app/grocery-list/[id].tsx` and its section components) · **Found by:** Hans, BUG-134 device pass, item 6.
- **Symptom, plainly:** the **"+ add item"** affordances that appear within the list sections **do nothing when tapped — anywhere on the list.** No sheet, no field, no error, no toast. The control looks live and is not.
- **The top-of-screen Add control WORKS**, with predictive search — so this is not the add route (`groceryLists.ts:763-782`), which was exercised successfully in the same pass. ⚠️ **It is a mobile-side handler defect, and it is NOT a regression from `8c234f0`: that commit touched only `api-server/src/lib` and `scripts/`, no mobile file.** Almost certainly pre-existing and never device-tested, since `app/**` sits outside the mobile test glob entirely (**D-WS9-164**), so no screen file is unit-tested end-to-end.
- ⚠️ **HANS'S PROPOSED FIX, and it is cheaper than wiring a second add surface:** have the in-list **"+ add item" controls anchor to the top add control** and open it in edit mode, rather than owning their own handler. **One add surface, several entry points.** Take this as the design intent unless Phase 0 finds a reason it cannot work.
- ✅ **FIXED `9e29910` (local, unpushed).** Root cause: **`onPress={() => {}}` at `[id].tsx:927`** — an empty handler — beside a comment claiming it *"focuses the top input via natural tab order."* ⚠️ **THAT IS A WEB CONCEPT THAT DOES NOTHING IN REACT NATIVE, AND THE COMMENT IS WHY NOBODY NOTICED: it described a mechanism that never existed.** The false comment was deleted rather than left beside the working code.
- **Built to Hans's anchor design** — a ref on the top `TextInput`; inline controls scroll-to-top and focus. **One add surface, several entry points.**
- ⚠️ **ORDER IS BEHAVIOUR, NOT STYLE — CC's OWN FINDING: scroll BEFORE focus, or the keyboard-avoiding view picks the position and can land the field UNDER the keyboard.** Pinned by guard A3, and Break O (focus before scroll) goes red.
- ⚠️ **BREAK N IS THE ONE THAT EARNS THE SET: it leaves the scroll firing and removes ONLY the focus — precisely what a *"did the handler run?"* assertion would wave through.** Tests assert the accumulated call list, not a boolean, so a short-circuit shows as a length mismatch.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED `9e29910`** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-117 · BUG-141 · BUG-152 · D-WS9-164.

### BUG-142 — A generated grocery row orders MORE than the sum of its own sources (6 vs 5)

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery generation (`groceryListAI.ts`) · **Found by:** Root-B Phase 0, **incidentally — it was hunting an older discrepancy that turned out not to exist.**
- **Symptom, plainly:** ⚠️ **a freshly generated list tells the shopper to buy one more tomato than any recipe in the plan wants.** List `641eb305`, row `0003f2b5`, `roma tomatoes`: **stored quantity 6**; sources Chunky Guacamole 1 + Tomato-Cucumber Salad 4 = **5**; an independent plan-side walk across all 4 meals also = **5**.
- **The rounding ladder is exonerated:** `roundNeedQuantity(5, "each")` returns 5 (`needQuantity.ts:40-53`).
- ⚠️ **CANDIDATE MECHANISM — PLAUSIBLE, EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED.** `groceryListAI.ts:517-521` takes the LLM's merged quantity **verbatim**. There is a **no-add guard** (item count must not increase) but **no guard that a merged quantity equals the sum of its inputs.** An LLM emitting 6 where inputs sum to 5 passes straight through. **CC did NOT confirm `roma tomatoes` entered the AI subset on that run.** Do not treat the mechanism as established.
- ⚠️ **THE SHAPE MATTERS MORE THAN THE MAGNITUDE, AND HANS'S TESTING RULING RAISES THE STAKES.** One tomato is trivial; a generation path that can inflate any merged quantity without a conservation check is not. **This is on the LIVE FORWARD PATH every new plan runs through** — and per D-WS9-186 fresh plans are now the test substrate, so a defect here pollutes every future device pass.
⚠️ **THE AI MECHANISM IS REFUTED (Phase 2) — CC REFUTED ITS OWN HYPOTHESIS.** List `641eb305` regenerated 2026-08-25T12:09:14Z with **ZERO LLM calls in the window** (last `grocery.generate_list` ever: 2026-08-24T22:46Z). The Sonnet pass is skipped when the AI subset is empty, so the row went through the **deterministic** path. `groceryListAI.ts:517-521` is not the mechanism.

⚠️ **THE FIRST SYSTEMIC SWEEP WAS WRONG AND CC CAUGHT IT.** First pass read **107/1844 = 5.8%**. Two flaws in its own reconstruction: it **ignored `recipeOverrideJson`** (225 rows sit on override plans; the consolidator works from the *effective* set), and the fractional deltas were **pre-Root-B ceiling artifacts**. Corrected, split at the Root-B commit: before **80/1328 = 6.0%** · after **11/308 = 3.6%**, of which 4 explained by the old ceil — **seven unexplained.** Cluster: `kosher salt` **+3 on six separate lists** · `smoked paprika` +3 · `fresh cilantro` +1.5 · `white onion` +1 · `roma tomatoes` +1 · `roma tomatoes` **−1** · `fresh cilantro` +0.25. **A constant +3 across lists carrying 1 to 10 sources is additive, not rounding.**

⚠️ **THE SAMPLE IS NOW KNOWN-CONTAMINATED — SEE BUG-148. Hans confirmed the api-server has been running under `dev` since BEFORE Root B landed (2026-08-24T20:54Z) and was never restarted, so every "post-Root-B" row was generated by a process executing PRE-Root-B code. THE 3.6% AND THE SEVEN ROWS MUST BE RE-MEASURED AFTER A RESTART.**

⚠️ **AND BUG-150 IS PLAUSIBLY THE SAME DEFECT IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION** — a recurring row and a meal need for the same ingredient collapsing into one row that carries only the recurring quantity. **This gains quantity; that loses it. One missing invariant, two signs.** Probe them together.

✅ **NO CONSERVATION GUARD EXISTS ANYWHERE — VERIFIED.** Two shapes, fully classified: the only two call sites (`groceryListAI.ts:418, :531`) both guard `items.length`, **a COUNT**. Eleven further hits were the English adjective *"conservative"*. ⚠️ **The proof is the invariant's own test title — `"item set + consolidation grouping unchanged; only quantity/unit/staple-flag differ"`. QUANTITY IS EXPLICITLY CARVED OUT OF THE ONLY NO-ADD INVARIANT IN THE CODEBASE.**

⚠️ **POST-RESTART RE-MEASURE (Probe 1, August 26): 0/59 DISAGREEMENTS — BUT THE SAMPLE WAS ONE LIST, AND THE CAVEAT WAS THE RIGHT CALL — SEE THE VERIFIED RESULT BELOW.** BUG-148's restart is verified (see its entry), and the seven unexplained rows did not reproduce. However 6 of the 7 post-restart lists carry `recipeOverrideJson` and are excluded entirely by the DishIngredient-only reconstruction, so the 0/59 rests on a single list — and coverage of the implicated ingredients was near-zero (fresh cilantro 0 rows measured, white onion 0, kosher salt 1, smoked paprika 1, roma tomatoes 1). At the prior 3.6% rate, P(0 of 59) ≈ 11%. **Unreproduced, not demonstrated fixed.** Probe Block 2 (F) runs an override-aware reconstruction so the excluded lists become measurable. The staple hypothesis was untestable this pass (0 disagreeing rows = 0/0; population base rate 11.9% staples for reference).

✅ **VERIFIED SURVIVING (Probe 2, August 27) — the override-aware reconstruction changed the answer.** Coverage **59 → 397 comparable rows** across the 7 post-restart lists (115 reconstructed *through* an override; excluded: 31 unit-converted, 24 no-source). **6 disagreements**, and the cluster narrowed from five ingredients to **one**:

```
fe29467b "kosher salt" stored 10.75 tsp  sum 7.75  delta +3     srcs=8  viaOverride=true   staple=true
bd4536c3 "kosher salt" stored 10.75 tsp  sum 7.75  delta +3     srcs=8  viaOverride=true   staple=true
117a0e67 "kosher salt" stored 10.75 tsp  sum 7.75  delta +3     srcs=8  viaOverride=true   staple=true
da1c036a "kosher salt" stored 10.75 tsp  sum 7.75  delta +3     srcs=8  viaOverride=true   staple=true
93a03e23 "kosher salt" stored 10.75 tsp  sum 7.75  delta +3     srcs=8  viaOverride=true   staple=true
858a5636 "kosher salt" stored 0.875 tbsp sum 1     delta -0.125 srcs=1  viaOverride=false  staple=true
```

⚠️ **THE OBVIOUS FALSE POSITIVE WAS TESTED AND REFUTED, WHICH IS WHY THIS COUNTS AS VERIFIED.** `STAPLE_VARIANT_TO_BASE` maps `kosher salt` → `salt`, so the consolidator merges across `ingredientId`s and an id-keyed sum could have been blind to sibling variants. Enumerating every salt-family ingredient across all 8 source dishes: **all 8 exact-id, family total 7.75 = exact-id total 7.75, stored 10.75.** The +3 is real.
- **The four previously-untested ingredients are now covered and CLEAN:** fresh cilantro 6 rows / 0 · white onion 6 / 0 · smoked paprika 7 / 0 · roma tomatoes 7 / 0 · **kosher salt 9 / 6**.
- **Staple correlation:** 6/6 disagreements are staples against an 11.9% population base rate.
✅ **MECHANISM VERIFIED (Phase 0, August 27) — FILE AND LINE. ⚠️ AND THE "AI IS EXONERATED" PREMISE IS REFUTED: `llm_call_logs` shows a `grocery.generate_list` call landing ≤1s before EVERY affected list, 7 for 7.** The earlier "zero LLM calls in the window" reading was wrong, and it had been load-bearing for two blocks.

**The arithmetic closes exactly.** Salt on those plans comes from **NINE** dishes, not eight: eight teaspoon sources summing to **7.75 tsp**, plus **Rigatoni at 1 tablespoon = 3 tsp**. 7.75 + 3 = **10.75**. ⚠️ **The reconstruction could not see the ninth because the stored row's own `GroceryListItemSource` set had already had it deleted — it was summing a provenance record from which the evidence had been removed.** The salt-family sweep was correct and still blind: the missing contribution was never in the family it swept.

**The chain, with lines:** `kosher salt` has `conversionRef` NULL and no code-table entry → **`groceryMerge.ts:154` groups on raw `canonicalName`, not `baseStapleName`**, so the variant never reaches base salt's `gramsPerCup: 273` → `ingredientConversions.ts:169-172` returns null for a volume unit with no `gramsPerCup` → `groceryMerge.ts:104-106` `mergeGroup` returns null and the deterministic merge **correctly declines** → **`groceryListAI.ts:277-281`, `partitionForAI` rule 3** routes any same-canonical/different-unit pair into the AI subset → **`groceryListAI.ts:445-450`** pops the first queue entry and, in the code's own comment, *"The un-popped sibling is simply dropped, which is what a merge means"* — **and its sources go with it.** ⚠️ **The only guards are `result.data.items.length > aiSubset.length` and `placed.length > items.length`. Both are COUNTS. Nothing checks quantity.**

⚠️ **THE NATURAL EXPERIMENT IS WHY THIS IS A FINDING AND NOT A STORY — ONE VARIABLE, ONE FAILURE.** Six canonicals on those plans appear in two units each and all need a cross-unit merge. **Five have conversion data and all five merged deterministically with quantity conserved** (`water` quart+tbsp · `fresh flat-leaf parsley` cup+tbsp · `fresh cilantro` cup+tbsp · `dijon mustard` tbsp+tsp · `honey` tbsp+tsp). **`kosher salt` (tbsp+tsp) is the only one the table cannot convert, and the only one that went wrong.** ⚠️ **`dijon mustard` and `honey` carry the IDENTICAL unit pair and were fine** — so "salt is weird," "tbsp↔tsp is hard," and "cross-unit merging is broken" are all excluded. The AI hand-off is the discriminator.

⚠️ **THE HEADLINE IS THE NONDETERMINISM, NOT THE +3.** Six runs, byte-identical input, **three different answers** — and identical input cannot produce three outputs from deterministic code:

| list | salt persisted | tsp ordered | needed | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ef8bfed5` | 6.75 tsp (9 srcs) | 6.75 | 6.75 | ✅ (no tbsp row existed) |
| `fe29467b` | 10.75 tsp **+ 1 tbsp** | 13.75 | 10.75 | 🔴 **OVER by 3 tsp (+28%)** |
| `858a5636` | 7.75 tsp + 0.875 tbsp | 10.375 | 10.75 | 🔴 UNDER by 0.375 |
| `bd4536c3` · `117a0e67` · `da1c036a` · `93a03e23` | 10.75 tsp | 10.75 | 10.75 | ✅ correct total |

⚠️ **THE FOUR "CORRECT" RUNS ARE CORRECT ONLY BECAUSE A NONDETERMINISTIC MODEL HAPPENED TO GET IT RIGHT — AND ALL SIX DESTROYED PROVENANCE** (a 9-source row persisting 8 sources). **A row whose source set no longer covers its own quantity is a live hazard for reconcile**, which reads exactly that — see BUG-126 and BUG-165. Corroborating tell: the six clone lists produce **65, 68, 69, 69, 70, 71 rows from identical input.**

- **Chat-Claude's recurring-item lead was REFUTED** — it was not a recurring quantity. It did, however, surface a real and separate collision: **BUG-164.**
- **Fix ruled (chat-Claude technical call, August 27) — BOTH LAYERS.** (1) Group on `baseStapleName` at `groceryMerge.ts:154` so staple variants inherit the base's conversion data — this kills the whole class deterministically. (2) Add a **quantity-conservation assertion** on the AI merge path whose failure mode is **leaving the rows UNMERGED**, never trusting the model's arithmetic. ⚠️ **Two ugly rows beat a silent 28% over-order**, and this is the systemic hole: the count-only guard is what let it through.
- ⚠️ **DENOMINATOR CAVEAT — chat-Claude's audit, owed to Phase 0 Q1a: THE 397 ROWS ARE PROBABLY NOT 397 INDEPENDENT OBSERVATIONS.** Five disagreements are byte-identical across five lists, and Probe 1 separately saw `White onion 1.25 ×6 lists`. The seven post-restart lists look like near-clones of one plan — which means the four clean ingredients are roughly **one** observation repeated, not six.
- ⚠️ **THE "ONE INVARIANT, TWO DIRECTIONS" HYPOTHESIS (with BUG-150) WAS WEAKENED, THEN RE-STRENGTHENED WITHIN THE HOUR — AND THE REVERSAL IS ITSELF THE LESSON.** A constant +3 tsp on staples first read as an additive *staple* rule rather than a conservation failure. **Then the reconstruction harness turned out to print the discriminator, and CC had truncated it from the report.** Its per-row line ends `staple=${...} recurring=${d.r.isRecurringItem}` — every quoted disagreement shows `staple=true` and **none shows the `recurring=` value.**
- ⚠️ **LEAD HYPOTHESIS, UNVERIFIED, AND CHEAP TO SETTLE (Phase 0 Q1): the +3 tsp is a RECURRING-ITEM quantity being ADDED to the meal need — 1 tbsp of salt, expressed in tsp.** That is **BUG-150's collapse running in the opposite direction**: same join, one arm losing quantity and the other gaining it. It also explains the staple enrichment without a staple rule — staples are exactly what users set as recurring items. **Re-run the harness and read the field that was cut before hunting anywhere else.**
- ⚠️ **PROCESS NOTE WORTH MORE THAN THE BUG: a field the harness already printed was dropped in summarisation, and the summary was then reasoned over as if complete.** Chat-Claude weakened a correct hypothesis on that truncated evidence. **When a report quotes tool output, check what the tool emitted that the report did not.**

🟡 **FIX BUILT AND COMMITTED `dbd389e` (unpushed) — August 27, 2026. TWO LAYERS, AND THE ONE CHAT-CLAUDE PRESCRIBED WAS THE INERT HALF.**

⚠️ **THE PRESCRIPTION WAS WRONG AND CC PROVED IT BY BREAKING IT.** The brief specified Layer 1 as a grouping-key change at `groceryMerge.ts:154`. **That change alone does nothing for the case that motivated it:** grouping puts both salt rows in a group keyed `salt`, but `groupConversion` resolves **per-member against each member's own canonical** — both are still `kosher salt`, both still miss, `mergeGroup` still returns null. **Break 2 stayed GREEN, which is what exposed it.** CC added the load-bearing half: a last-resort fallback in `groupConversion` to the **base staple's** code-table row (density is a property of the substance — kosher salt is salt, so `gramsPerCup: 273` applies), narrowed by construction because it consults `STAPLE_VARIANT_TO_BASE`, an exact-string map, so `garlic salt` / `celery salt` never inherit it. ⚠️ **Shipping the prescription alone would have been a no-op believed to be a fix.**

**Measured on the live consolidator, all 7 plans, before → after:** salt `7.75 tsp (8 srcs) + 1 tbsp (1 src)` → **`10.75 tsp, 9 srcs`** · DUP-CANON lines **6 → 0** · consolidated rows **488 → 482** · AI subset **66 → 56**. **Salt no longer reaches the AI at all.**

**Layer 2 — the conservation guard (`groceryListAI.ts`).** For any canonical the model was handed >1 row of, the returned total must equal the given total, compared **in grams** through the same conversion resolution the merge uses. ⚠️ **Failure mode is REFUSAL: the AI's rows for that canonical are discarded whole and the consolidated rows ship UNMERGED at their original indices.** Rows returned unchanged are exempt. Tolerance **0.5% relative** — ⚠️ **calibrated to the two observed defects (+28%, −3.5%), NOT to a population; CC flagged this itself.**
- **Guard removed, per §10 replace-don't-layer:** the global `placed.length > items.length` check — a second guard on the same concern as the local no-add check, whose own comment conceded it was unreachable, **and which is now actively wrong** (a legitimate refusal restores rows and could fail an entire generation for a safe outcome). The local `result.data.items.length > aiSubset.length` guard **stays** — it polices a genuinely different failure (the model inventing items) and caught a test scenario before the conservation guard could.
- **Mutation testing, both directions (§27.4/§27.5):** every break proved applied by grepping the mutated text, every restore verified by `sha256sum -c`. Break 3 (`Math.abs` → over-only) went red on the under-count test; break 4 (under-only) went red on the over-count test. ⚠️ **Break 2 stayed green and was a TEST finding** — the suite pinned the conversion fallback but nothing pinned the grouping key; a two-variant test (`sea salt 1 tsp + kosher salt 1 tbsp → 4 tsp`) was added and goes red.
- **Blast radius enumerated:** **9 of 1,570 Ingredient rows (0.57%)** have a base-staple name differing from their canonical — the olive-oil, salt and black-pepper families. On the 7 live plans the key change forms only two new cross-canonical groups and **neither merges** (`salt` = kosher 6.75 tsp + salt 1 pinch → pinch unconvertible → passes through; `olive oil` = 3 rows all tablespoon → single unit → `mergeGroup` not called).

⚠️ **NOT PROVEN END-TO-END, AND THE REASON MATTERS.** The reconstruction harness reads **persisted** provenance and no new list was generated, so **before and after are necessarily identical (6 disagreements both times)** — the number cannot move without a regeneration. ⚠️ **Those 6 are BUG-165 rendered visible: the row's quantity is right (10.75) but its 8 surviving sources account for only 7.75 — off by exactly the dropped tablespoon.** The paired direct check (live consolidator, sources-account-for-quantity) reads **432/432 before and 426/426 after, 0 failures both** — the deterministic consolidator was always self-consistent; **the destruction was entirely on the AI/persist path.** **The device pass on a freshly generated list is the proof.**

**Suites:** api 1670 / 1668 pass / 0 fail / 2 pre-existing skips · mobile 1206 / 1206 / 0 fail · `tsc` exit 0 both sides, redirected to files.

✅ **PROVEN END-TO-END ON A LIVE REGENERATION (Hans, device pass, August 27) — this is the evidence the harness structurally could not produce.** A freshly generated 69-item list renders **`1 container (26 oz) Kosher salt · Pantry Staple · (11 teaspoon)`** — **ONE row, sourced from five meals.** Pre-fix this was two rows and a silent double-count.
✅ **AND THE LAYER-2 GUARD FIRED LIVE, CORRECTLY, ON A DIFFERENT INGREDIENT:** `hot sauce` appeared as `1 bottle (5 oz) hot sauce (1 teaspoon)` **and** `1 bottle (5 oz) hot sauce (1 tablespoon)` — same canonical, two units, unconvertible, **merge refused, both rows shipped unmerged.** ⚠️ **HANS REPORTED THESE AS IDENTICAL DUPLICATE LINES AND THEY ARE NOT — the units differ.** The rows are adjacent and near-identical at a glance, which is exactly why the device script flagged this shape in advance as *"guard fired, not a failure."* ⚠️ **WORTH KEEPING AS A UX SIGNAL: if the product owner reads the safe failure mode as a bug, a real user will too. The refusal may need to say why.**
- **Pushed** `9e29910..dbd389e`, August 27, 2026.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED, device-verified and PUSHED (`dbd389e`)** · **Priority:** **P1** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-126 · BUG-148 · BUG-150 · BUG-164 · BUG-165 · BUG-167 · BUG-168 · **D-WS9-189** · D-WS9-186.

### BUG-144 — "1 garlic cloves (1 each)" — the singular guard misses when the pack residue doesn't name the item

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list detail, count path · **Found by:** Root-B Phase 0, live on list `22117b24`.
- **Symptom:** a count of 1 renders the plural noun. **The Root-D singular guard only fires when the pack residue NAMES the item** (`packNamesItem`); where it does not — `"1 head of garlic"` does not name `garlic cloves` — the other branch calls `pluralizeIngredientName(name, q)` and the guard never runs.
- **Same family as BUG-130**, which fixed singularisation at a count of 1 and shipped a device-verified pass. ⚠️ **This is the branch that fix did not cover — a count-of-1 guard has to be asserted on EVERY branch, not on the one that produced the report.**
- ⚠️ **The garlic fold removes this INSTANCE for free** (the row becomes `1 head Garlic`) **but not the DEFECT** — any row whose pack residue doesn't name the item hits the same branch.
- ✅ **FIXED (local, unpushed) — AND THE BRIEF WAS TOO NARROW.** CC extended to the **no-pack branch**, which had the identical shape and **carried 9 of the 10 live rows**: `"1 bananas"`, `"1 pet treats"`, `"1 Nespresso pods"`. The named branch carried **one**. Device-verified items 1, 3, 7.
- **`COUNT_NOUN_SINGULARS` is DERIVED by inverting `COUNT_NOUN_PLURALS`, not authored, so the directions cannot drift** — 583 of 584 changed names round-trip.
- ⚠️ **BREAK G STAYED GREEN, AND THAT WAS A FINDING ABOUT THE CODE.** `INVARIANT_NAME_NOUNS` was **dead** — all 14 entries already fail `isPluralWord`. Deleted rather than left as reassuring-looking dead code.
- ⚠️ **THAT DELETION EXPOSED A MIS-STEM CC HAD ITSELF INTRODUCED — `molasses` → `molass`.** Rather than guess at English mass nouns it scanned all **1,725** catalog names for the `-es` reversal: **3 hits, one wrong.** `NOT_A_PLURAL` is **sized to that measurement — one entry.** ✅ **Device item 7 confirmed live: `1 bottle (10 oz) pomegranate molasses (1 tbsp)`.**
- ⚠️ **ROOT D's GUARD D2 IS NOW INCONSISTENT WITH ITS OWN RATIONALE** — it accepted `"1 roma tomatoes"` **because no stemmer existed. One now does.** CC pinned D2 and escalated rather than quietly rerouting. One-line change, unruled.
- ⚠️ **THE PLURAL DIRECTION IS NOT FULLY FIXED — SEE BUG-149 (`"2 apple"`).**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED for the singular direction — 10 live order lines corrected** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-125 · BUG-130 · BUG-143 · BUG-149.

### BUG-145 — Full-replace regeneration hard-deletes grocery rows and discards user quantity overrides

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** grocery list regeneration · **Found by:** Root-B Phase 0, **while trying and failing to recover evidence that had been silently erased.**
- **What happened:** lists `641eb305` (12:09Z) and `c6ff50af` (14:05Z) regenerated on August 25. `lastGeneratedAt` moved and **every row id is new** — a **full replace, not a delta reconcile.** Hans's purchase-quantity overrides from the BUG-134 device pass were destroyed, along with the BUG-134 sheet's row ids.
- ✅ **RULED NOT A DEFECT (Hans, August 25, 2026) — see D-WS9-186.** Verbatim: *"if a user added a quantity on the grocery list, then added a meal to their plan and then goes back to the grocery list, they get a toast 'updated to match your plan' and it's aligned… it's a lot of work and effort to try to remember they added an extra tomato and then add that back in. in my opinion not worth it."* ⚠️ **The list is downstream of the plan; when the plan changes the list is rebuilt, and overrides are legitimately overwritten.**
- ⚠️ **TWO NARROW RESIDUALS SURVIVE THE RULING, AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME QUESTION:**
  1. **Does the "updated to match your plan" toast EXIST?** Hans's ruling assumes the overwrite is *announced*. **Unprobed.** A silent overwrite is not what was ruled.
  2. **Can a regeneration fire when the plan has NOT changed?** Hans: *"I probably (not for sure) regenerated those."* The ruling covers plan-changed rebuilds. **Churning rows with no plan change is a different defect and remains open.**
- ⚠️ **SECOND-ORDER COST, ALREADY PAID ONCE: this destroyed evidence for an open investigation mid-flight.** Any probe reading a live grocery row is racing a regeneration it cannot see. **Snapshot before probing.**
- **Status:** ✅ **CLOSED as ruled-expected — two narrow residuals tracked above** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-134 · BUG-142 · BUG-148 · D-WS9-180 · D-WS9-186.

### BUG-148 — The api-server was running PRE-Root-B code; every "post-Root-B" measurement is contaminated

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** api-server runtime · **Found by:** CC, Phase 2, **as a self-flagged limit on its own conclusions** — *"If the running server is not on current source, none of Phase 1 is live either."*
- **Symptom:** generations dated 2026-08-25 still showed the **old ceil** (`Lemon 4.5 → 5`), removed by Root B on 2026-08-24T20:54Z.
- ✅ **CAUSE CONFIRMED BY HANS, August 25, 2026: the server is running under `dev` (correct per §6) but has been up since BEFORE Root B landed and was NEVER RESTARTED.** The process is executing the code it loaded at startup. ⚠️ **`dist/index.mjs` was excluded early — dated 2026-05-31, predating `needQuantity.ts` — so the stale-`dist` variant of §6 was NOT the mechanism. It is the simpler one: a long-lived process outliving its source.**
- ⚠️ **§6 ALREADY NAMES HALF OF THIS AND MISSES THE OTHER HALF.** *"Run with `dev`, never `build`"* and *"don't assume the dev server has fresh code — restart after meaningful changes."* **Hans did the first correctly and the second is the one that bit.** ⚠️ **Doing the right thing at start-up does not keep being right; a `dev` server is only as fresh as its last restart.**
- ⚠️ **WHAT IT CONTAMINATES:** (1) **BUG-142's entire post-Root-B sample** — the 3.6% and the seven unexplained rows were generated by pre-Root-B code and **must be re-measured**; (2) **any fresh plan generated for testing**, which per D-WS9-186 is now the agreed substrate. ⚠️ **Client render work (BUG-143, BUG-144) runs through Metro, NOT the api-server, so the device pass on those remains VALID.**
- **Remedy:** restart, regenerate one list, confirm the ceil is gone.
- ✅ **REMEDY RUN AND VERIFIED (Probe 1 C0, August 26) — by a per-row discriminator, not the ambiguous aggregate.** The old code ceiled any quantity not in `MEASURED_UNITS`, so a fractional count-unit quantity is impossible under it. All 8 fractional count-unit rows on the day's 7 lists match current code (`Yellow onion` 0.5, `Red onion` 0.25, `White onion` 1.25 ×6 lists — each stored exactly at its reconstructed source sum), 0 match the old ceil. **The server is on current source; post-restart measurements are clean.**
- **Status:** ✅ **RESOLVED — restart run and gate-verified; no code change (operational defect)** · **Priority:** **P1** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-142 · BUG-150 · D-WS9-186. ⚠️ **The §6 amendment this earned ("a `dev` server is only as fresh as its last restart") is owed to working agreements at WS9 close.**

### BUG-149 — `"2 apple"` — the PLURAL direction fails at counts above 1

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** client render, count path · **Found by:** Hans, Root-B device pass item 6, on a **freshly generated** row.
- **Symptom, plainly:** a list showed `1 apple`; Hans added one more apple to a meal; the row updated to **`2 apple`.** ⚠️ **This is the exact criterion item 6 was written to catch — a singular at a count above 1 — and it fires.**
- ⚠️ **HANS MARKED IT "PASS" AND IT IS RECORDED HERE AS A FAILURE, DELIBERATELY.** He judged it low-priority and he may well be right about priority; but it is the inverse of the defect BUG-144 just fixed, on the path BUG-144 just changed, and calling it a pass would bury it.
- ⚠️ **PROVENANCE UNKNOWN AND THAT IS THE FIRST QUESTION, NOT AN ASIDE.** CC's 10-row before/after measurement covered **rows that already existed**. This row was **generated fresh during the device pass**, so it may sit on a path the measurement never touched. **Determine whether it is a BUG-144 regression or pre-existing before designing a fix.**
- **Note:** CC reported that of 584 names the singulariser changes, **583 round-trip** through the pluraliser. **One does not. Check whether that one is `apple` before looking anywhere else.**
- ✅ **PRE-EXISTING, NOT A BUG-144 REGRESSION — PROVEN, NOT ARGUED.** CC ran the **pre-BUG-144 module on the same inputs** and got `"2 apple"` **byte-identically.** That was the first question and it is now closed with evidence.
- ⚠️ **CHAT-CLAUDE'S LEAD WAS WRONG, AND THE REASON IS WORTH KEEPING.** The single name that fails the singulariser→pluraliser round-trip is **`whole wheat pita breads`** (because `bread` is an invariant noun), **not `apple`.** `pluralizeIngredientName("apple", 2)` returns `"apples"` correctly. ⚠️ **THE BRANCH SIMPLY NEVER CALLED IT.** Rule 1's **residue-reuse path** returns `${q} ${residue}` verbatim, on the standing assumption that a stored pack is *"authored and correctly pluralized"* — **true for `"4 roma tomatoes"`, false for any pack authored singular.**
- **Live effect across 2,537 rows:** `2 shallot` → `2 shallots` · `2 yellow bell pepper` → `2 yellow bell peppers` · plus `apple`.
- ⚠️ **MUTATION TESTING SIMPLIFIED THE FIX RATHER THAN MERELY VALIDATING IT — THE THIRD CONSECUTIVE BLOCK IN WHICH A GREEN BREAK FOUND SOMETHING.** CC's first draft guarded `q > 1 && !isPluralWord(residue)`. **Deleting EITHER condition left the suite green** — `pluralizeIngredientName` already no-ops at ≤1, and `pluralizeNoun` already no-ops on a plural word. **Both conditions were protection-shaped code pinning nothing.** The branch is now **one unconditional call**, and Break K (bare residue) is red on 4 guards.
- ⚠️ **CC ALSO CAUGHT ITS OWN BAD FIXTURE:** guard P4 used name `"bay leaf"` against residue `"leaf"`, which fails `residueNamesItem` and **silently exercised the other branch.** A fixture that tests the wrong branch is indistinguishable from one that passes.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED `0fdfda0` (local, unpushed)** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-130 · BUG-144.

### BUG-152 — Adding a custom recurring item in preferences saves, but renders no chip and gives no confirmation

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** preferences (Home → profile initials → preferences) · **Found by:** Hans, device pass item 1. **Hans explicitly asked that this be logged rather than chased.**
- **Symptom:** adding a custom recurring item (`lime`) **persisted correctly but produced no visible chip and no feedback**, so there was no way to be confident it had saved. Confirmed saved only by generating a list afterwards.
- **Proposed fix (Hans):** render a new chip on add.
- ⚠️ **Same family as BUG-140 — a control that acts saved/live while giving the user nothing back.** Worth fixing in the same mobile pass.
- ✅ **FIXED `ca9333b` (local, unpushed) — ⚠️ AND NEITHER CANDIDATE MECHANISM WAS RIGHT. NOT a render bug, NOT a stale query.** `value` is a **local `useState` buffer** (`preferences.tsx:72`) updated **synchronously** by `update()`. ⚠️ **THE ITEM DID RENDER IMMEDIATELY — as a row at the TOP of the section, while the user was looking at the input at the BOTTOM.** The only other confirmation was an **800ms-debounced toast.**
- ⚠️ **NOTHING WAS BROKEN. THE FEEDBACK WAS SIMPLY NOWHERE NEAR THE ACTION.** ⚠️ **This is the most transferable finding of the block: a user report of *"it didn't save"* was actually *"the confirmation was off-screen."* Both chat-Claude's hypotheses (missing render, un-invalidated query) assumed a technical failure; the defect was spatial.**
- ⚠️ **AND `lime` COULD NEVER HAVE BEEN A CHIP — the chip row was HARDCODED to the eight `COMMON_RECURRING_ITEMS`.** Custom items were structurally excluded, not failing to render.
- **Not new duplication:** a common item selected from the chips **already** rendered twice (selected chip + removable row). **Custom items were the inconsistent case; all recurring items now get the same treatment.**
- ⚠️ **NO INVALIDATION TEST, DELIBERATELY — there is no refetch on this path to assert, so writing one would have reproduced exactly the vacuous `queryCache.build()`-with-no-observer shape §27.5 records.** Declining to write a test is the right call when the thing it would assert does not exist.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED `ca9333b`** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-140 · BUG-141 · BUG-153 · BUG-154.

### BUG-153 — Plan review and meal-selection screens show only the meal title, no subtext

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** plan review screen (the "use my preferences" path) and the select-meals-for-a-plan screen · **Found by:** Hans, device pass feedback.
- **Symptom:** meals render as **title only**. A title alone is often not enough to choose between meals, and the subtext already exists elsewhere in the product.
- **Hans:** apply the same principle to **both** screens, not just the one where it was noticed.
- ✅ **FIXED `8df96df` (local, unpushed) — TWO SURFACES, TWO DIFFERENT CAUSES.** `PlanReviewMealRow`: `ReviewPlanMealRow` had **no `description` field**, so `reviewPlanAdapter` had nowhere to put it. `AddMealsSheet`: ⚠️ **the ADAPTER was the bug — `mealListItemToSummary` DROPPED it. The server had been sending the line the whole time.**
- **`MealSummary` consumers enumerated before widening** — `plan/[id].tsx`, `AddDishToMealSheet`, `AddMealsSheet`, `SwapMealSheet`, `mealSort`, `planDetailOptimistic`, `mealListItemToSummary`. Field is optional, so every constructor that omits it still compiles.
- **Render pattern reused verbatim from `MealRow.tsx:81-85`** (§27.2). ⚠️ **NO FALLBACK, AND BREAK T PROVES IT — substituting `"No description available"` goes red.** A fallback would have hidden BUG-156 and made the writer gap undetectable on device.
- ⚠️ **A THIRD FACE OF BUG-156, FOUND HERE: `wizardDraftReviewAdapter` leaves `description` undefined because THE WIZARD DRAFT PAYLOAD HAS NO SUCH FIELD TO READ.** So the gap is not only *"the generator doesn't populate it"* — **the draft payload shape does not carry it either.** CC declined to paper over it.
- ⚠️ **BUG-157 sweep sites, recorded so the eventual token fix finds them: `PlanReviewMealRow.tsx:358` and `AddMealsSheet.tsx:341`.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED `8df96df`** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-152 · BUG-154 · BUG-156 · BUG-157.

### BUG-154 — The "Anything else?" placeholder on preferences is too low-contrast; an outside tester missed the feature entirely

- **Date:** August 25, 2026 · **Surface:** preferences, free-text field · **Found by:** ⚠️ **a friend testing the app — the first external-user finding in this arc.**
- **Symptom:** the field's suggested text (`e.g. "no cilantro", "lower sodium"`) is faint enough that the tester **did not see the option and reported it as MISSING.**
- **Fix (Hans):** darken the text — *"just a bit darker, not super standout."*
- ⚠️ **WEIGHT THIS ABOVE ITS SIZE. A feature that exists and is invisible reads to a user as a feature that does not exist**, and this is the only report in the arc from someone outside the build loop.
- ✅ **FIXED `0fdfda0` (local, unpushed).** `neutral[600]` `#8A8474` measured **3.7278:1** on the white card — clears 3:1 for non-text, **fails 4.5:1 AA**. Moved to `neutral[700]` `#6B5E4D` = **6.2999:1**. `neutral[800]` would have been **10.2697:1** — rejected as too loud against Hans's *"not super standout."*
- ⚠️ **RATIOS REPORTED AT FULL PRECISION DELIBERATELY.** `2.9966:1` prints as `3.00` at two decimals and reads as passing; this project has been bitten by exactly that.
- ⚠️ **THE FIX IS SCOPED TO ONE FIELD BUT THE TOKEN IS SHARED — AND THIS IS BIGGER THAN PLACEHOLDERS.** `neutral[600]` is the **LOCKED muted-text token** and roughly **20 other placeholders** read it across groceries, plans, profile, dish-builder, meal-builder and import. ⚠️ **It is also used for real body TEXT — `MealRow.tsx:81-85` renders meal descriptions at `neutral[600]`, which means that text sits at 3.73:1 and is BELOW AA today.** So the question is not only *"are placeholders too faint"* but *"is the muted token being used as text where it cannot pass."* **Disposition unruled — see BUG-157.**
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED for the named field** · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-152 · BUG-153 · BUG-156 · BUG-157.
