### BUG-207 — The component pool's appended parent row inherits the CHILD's purchase pack

- **Date:** September 5, 2026 · **Surface:** `poolComponentNeeds` — the grocery component-pooling pass · **Found by:** CC, D-WS9-189 A2 Phase 1b (dry-run harness).
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — D-WS9-189 A3, September 6, 2026, and it was fixed BEFORE the gate opened, as required.** **`beba57d`** (A3 — hash confirmed from the repo's own reflog on September 7, 2026, not from a report). The appended parent row is now built from **parent facts only**. ⚠️ **CC's correction to this entry's framing, and it is the durable part: the inherited PACK was the smaller half of the damage — the inherited `ingredientId` was what made it durable, because a null pack on a row carrying an id is a `fillPurchaseSizesWithWriteBack` miss, and that helper writes Haiku's guess onto the CHILD's catalog row.** · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-208** · **BUG-200** · **D-WS9-189** · D-WS9-220 · BUG-171.
- **Symptom:** when the pass finds no existing parent row it seeds a new one from a CHILD row, carrying `purchaseUnit` / `purchaseDisplay` across. Five live cases measured: `1 bottle (64 oz) orange juice (3 tbsp)` → **`1 bottle (64 oz) orange (1 each)`** · `2 lb bag broccoli florets (5 cup)` → **`2 lb bag broccoli (2 heads)`** · `1 can (7 oz) adobo sauce` → **`1 can (7 oz) chipotle chile in adobo`**.
- ⚠️ **THIS IS A WRONG INSTRUCTION, NOT A COSMETIC DEFECT — it tells the shopper to buy a bottle of oranges.**
- ⚠️ **INERT TODAY.** `COMPONENT_POOLING_ENABLED = false`, and A2 proved the held pass is row-for-row identical to synonym-only across all 138 would-be pools. **The defect ships the moment the gate opens, so it is a gate prerequisite, not a follow-up.**
- **Fix:** the appended parent row takes the PARENT ingredient's own purchase pack, or none at all. **Never the child's.**

### BUG-208 — `canonicalUnitToken` does not alias `head` ↔ `each`, and that is what actually blocks BUG-200

- **Date:** September 5, 2026 · **Surface:** `canonicalUnitToken`; garlic rows on the grocery list · **Found by:** CC, D-WS9-189 A2 Phase 1b.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — D-WS9-189 A3, September 6, 2026 — BUT NOT AS THIS ENTRY SPECIFIED, AND THE ENTRY'S ROOT-CAUSE CLAIM WAS WRONG.** **`beba57d`** (A3 — hash confirmed from the repo's own reflog on September 7, 2026, not from a report). 🔴 **CC REFUSED the `canonicalUnitToken` head↔each alias with reasons, and was right:** that map is documented **keys-only** with an explicit no-speculation rule (`heads` has zero live rows in `dish_ingredients`), and it feeds `bucketKeyOf` and `groceryReconcile.matchKey` — **widening it would make stored rows reconcile as delete+add.** ⚠️ **The real defect was in the POOL'S TOP-UP FILTER: a parent row in a unit the basis cannot reach made it APPEND rather than MATCH — `fresh cilantro` in cups against a `bunch` basis produced two rows both named "fresh cilantro". Measured with the gate open before the guard: 7 new duplicate-name rows across 53 lists.** It now declines and prints. **Five-line change, no blast radius.** ⚠️ **AND THIS ENTRY'S CLAIM THAT IT WAS BUG-200'S ROOT CAUSE IS ALSO FALSE — BUG-200 was blocked by garlic being four catalog rows, which the hand map addresses. Chat-Claude wrote both claims from a prior CC's characterisation without an existence check (§8.1).** · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-200** · **BUG-207** · **D-WS9-220** · D-WS9-189 · BUG-123.
- **Symptom:** a `head of garlic` row carries unit `head`; the `garlic head` component pool's basis is `each`. The tokens differ, so the pool appends a SECOND row instead of topping up the existing one. `mergeConvertibleGroups` cannot rescue it — no shared unit, no shared dimension, and `garlic head` / `head of garlic` / `whole garlic head` all carry no `subUnit`; only the row named `garlic` has one.
- ⚠️ **TWO DIFFERENT STATES HERE AND THEY MUST NOT BE CONFLATED.** **Today, gated:** lists `8908a51a` and `84ab7e43` emit two garlic lines — that is BUG-200 exactly as first reported, unchanged by A2. **Ungated, measured in the dry run:** the pool computes the head-equivalent totals **correctly** (5 heads and 3 heads — precisely D-WS9-220's arithmetic) **and still emits two lines.**
- 🔴 **SO THE ARITHMETIC WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM, AND OPENING THE COMPONENT GATE DOES NOT CLOSE BUG-200. THIS DOES.** Any plan that assumes the pool alone resolves the garlic double-line is wrong.

### BUG-209 — `pickRepresentative` silently re-picks the purchase PACK, not just the name

- **Date:** September 6, 2026 · **Surface:** `pickRepresentative` in the grocery consolidation path · **Found by:** CC, D-WS9-189 A2 regression probe §4a.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `e43cfbb`, September 6, 2026, local at time of writing.** `pickPackBasis` in `groceryMerge.ts`, with `finishMerge` settling the pack in ONE place for all four merge branches — **all four carried the defect, and a fifth branch can no longer forget.** `pickRepresentative` untouched: the NAME rule was never the problem. · **Priority:** **P2** · **Cross-ref:** **D-WS9-221** · **BUG-171** · **D-WS9-189** · BUG-136 · BUG-124 · D-WS9-217.
- 🔴 **THE ORIGINAL "11 of 53 lists" FIGURE OVERSTATED THE SHOPPER-VISIBLE BLAST RADIUS BY ROUGHLY 5×, AND CHAT-CLAUDE RELAYED IT TO HANS AS THE HEADLINE.** Measured over pre-merge groups on all 53 lists: **36 divergent-pack groups unwired, 48 wired — but 38 of the 48 involve a universal staple, whose pack BUG-171 suppresses at render.** **The shopper-visible population is 10 group instances across 5 families** (red pepper flakes ×4, lemon ×2, jalapeño ×2, parmesan ×1, unsalted butter ×1). ⚠️ **Every one of the 6 "representative loses a size" cases is black pepper — a staple. The `extra-virgin olive oil` 25.5 oz → 17 oz flip that headlined the September 6 report is ALSO a staple and was never rendered.** **The fix is still right; the number was a measurement of computed rows, not of rendered ones, and nobody asked which (§27.6).**
- **Measured:** 11 of 53 lists differ between `c06b208` and `6a82218`. **Every one of the 11 is this, and NONE of it is the relation reader** — `pickRepresentative` (shortest normalized name wins) runs unconditionally on any group the hand map already folded, so it needed no relation index to ship. `extra-virgin olive oil / 1 bottle (25.5 oz)` → **`olive oil / 1 bottle (17 oz)`** on 11 lists; `ground black pepper / 1 container (2.3 oz)` → **`black pepper / 1 container`** on 2.
- 🔴 **THE NAME CHANGE IS DEFENSIBLE; THE PACK CHANGE IS THE DEFECT.** The surviving row carries a different `ingredientId`, so it resolves a different catalog row's `conversionRef` — the bottle shrinks 25.5 → 17 oz and the pepper loses its residue. **Quantities, units, sections and row counts are identical on all 11**; only name and pack move.
- ⚠️ **LATENT, NOT INERT.** Stored rows still carry the old names; **the change lands the first time each of those 11 lists is regenerated or reconciled.** A live exposure with a delay, not a hypothetical.
- **The fix is decoupling: the representative NAME and the purchase PACK must be derived independently.** ⚠️ **Which row supplies the pack is UNRULED.** The commit argued shortest-name on readability grounds and named the BUG-136 side benefit; it did not measure the pack consequence.

### BUG-211 — `11 garlic cloves` tells the shopper to buy `1 head of garlic`

- **Date:** September 6, 2026 · **Surface:** the Haiku purchase-pack fill; catalog row `garlic cloves` (`7835b896`) · **Found by:** CC, A2 regression probe §4e.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — D-WS9-189 A3, September 6, 2026.** **`beba57d`** (A3 — hash confirmed from the repo's own reflog on September 7, 2026, not from a report). `11 garlic cloves (1 head of garlic)` is now part of **`3 heads Garlic (25 cloves)`**. ⚠️ **It additionally required the subUnit branch to stop reading a bare `each` as a third unit** — `garlic cloves` is authored in `each`, so `childSet` was `{clove, each}` and the group was refused. **Scope of that widening, measured: exactly ONE of 1,569 catalog rows carries a `subUnit` ladder, so it cannot reach anything but garlic.** · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-208** · **BUG-200** · **D-WS9-220** · BUG-207 · D-WS9-189.
- **Measured:** the row renders `11 garlic cloves (11 each)` with `purchaseDisplay = "1 head of garlic"`. **11 cloves needs 2 heads.** The ingredient carries **no `subUnit`**, so the head↔clove ladder never fired and the pack came from the Haiku fill instead.
- ⚠️ **THE WEEK'S TRUE DEMAND IS 25 CLOVES → 3 HEADS by D-WS9-220.** The two garlic lines **PARTITION** the demand across six dishes and do not double-count: 14 cloves resolved to catalog row `garlic` (which has `subUnit {parent: head, perParent: 10}`, so `ceil(1.4) = 2 heads` is correct for its own row), 11 to catalog row `garlic cloves`. **The shopper buys 2 + 1 = 3 heads, which happens to be the right answer — coincidence stacked on an under-buy, not a working ladder.**
- 🔴 **WIRING THE SYNONYM READER DOES NOT FIX THIS.** Measured: `garlic → garlic cloves` is labelled **`component`** (yield 10 clove, `coHarvestable = false`, high), and under the FULL index the two group keys still differ.

### BUG-215 — A fold can land the merged line on a catalog row that has lost the subUnit ladder, silently under-buying

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `resolvePurchaseFields` → `scalePurchaseForSubUnit` reads only the SURVIVOR row's `conversionRef`; conversion payloads are not merged across a fold, and BUG-142's conservation guard checks quantity, not survivor identity · **Found by:** CC, read-only 4-list audit.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `a9fb3b9`, September 7, 2026 (local), by the PAYLOAD shape (b), not the identity shape.** CC's reasoning, and it is the durable part: identity would change the name the shopper reads in order to fix a pack — **the coupling BUG-209 deliberately severed** — and it cannot help where the ladder reaches the group through a name fallback rather than a member row. New `withGroupLadder` copies only `subUnit` + its `purchaseUnit`, only onto a row that has none, never over a row's own; densities untouched. Three deliberate breaks, all red. ⚠️ **AND CC REFINED THE PHASE 0 ANSWER RATHER THAN REPEATING IT: the deterministic path PERMITS the defect but today's data does not exercise it** — `pickRepresentative` ranks on name length and `garlic` (6 chars) is the shortest of the five folding names, so the ladder-bearing row wins whenever present, and `foldMetadata` already copies a member's ref onto a null winner. **The residual hole is a winner carrying a ref that has no `subUnit` — ordinary, since 236 rows carry a ref and exactly one has a ladder.** · **Priority:** **P1** · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-217** (found beside it and larger) · **BUG-214** · BUG-211 · BUG-200 · D-WS9-220 · **D-WS9-225** · BUG-142.
- **Symptom, measured:** list `cb5c8f6e` · `garlic` · need **16 cloves** · pack **"1 head of garlic"** → buy 1, need ~2. Three catalog rows share the fold key `garlic`: `garlic` carries `subUnit {parent: head, perParent: 10}`; `garlic cloves` and `garlic head` carry no subUnit. When `garlic` survives, 25 cloves → "3 heads" ✅ (list `167a144d`). When `garlic cloves` survives, the ladder returns null and the stored pack prints against the clove need. **`scalePurchaseForSubUnit(conv_garlic, 16, "clove")` returns `{2, "2 heads"}` — the right answer exists one row over.**
- ⚠️ **Sits in the "opaque" bucket, not the under-buy bucket, precisely because the survivor lacks the field that would make it computable** — the audit's zero-under-buy figure is honest and this row is still an under-buy.
- **The fix shape (CC to test, licence to refuse):** either prefer as survivor the row carrying a `subUnit` ladder (identity), or merge `conversionRef` payloads across the fold so the ladder travels with the group. **Adjacent to D-WS9-220 but distinct — the spec is right; survivor selection defeats it.**
- ⚠️ **UNVERIFIED HYPOTHESIS (CC's):** observed on the AI-merge path only; **if `mergeConvertibleGroups` can ever pick a non-ladder row as survivor of a same-unit group, the deterministic path has it too.** Check the representative rule in the fix block.

### BUG-216 — The size-hint parser does not alias `count` to `each`, so `1 package (12 count)` prints against a need of 36 tortillas

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** mobile `lib/api/grocery.ts` — `packSizeHint` returns `{12, "count"}`, `normalizeUnitToken("count")` ≠ `each`, `packsToCoverNeed` returns null and the stored pack prints verbatim · **Found by:** CC, read-only 4-list audit.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `a9fb3b9`, September 7, 2026 (local).** `count: "each"` added; the break printed the under-buy verbatim (`1 package (12 count) corn tortillas` where 3 packages are needed). ⚠️ **PREMISE CORRECTION FROM CC: the file is `lib/format/grocery.ts`, not `lib/api/grocery.ts`.** ✅ **AND IT REFUSED THE ADJACENT ALIASES AND WAS RIGHT:** `ct` / `pcs` / `pieces` / `piece` are absent from `UNIT_ALIASES`, the table's stated convention is *"only what the live data actually uses"*, and **BUG-174's scope note rules against it directly** — `whole`, `piece` and `ct` resolve grams via `gramsPerEach`, *"which is a statement about density lookup, not about the words being spellings of each other… each therefore stays each."* **Adding them would contradict a live ruling to fix nothing measured.** · **Priority:** **P3** · **Cross-ref:** BUG-125 · BUG-124 · BUG-174.
- **Symptom:** `corn tortillas` · need 36 each · `1 package (12 count)` → 1 package shown; correct is 3. Present on `167a144d` and `bd29f91a`. **One entry in `UNIT_ALIASES` (`count → each`) makes it 3 packages** — it is one alias short of Root A's own fix. ⚠️ **Prove it with a break (§27.4): the test must read the live alias table.**

### BUG-219 — Password-reset and email-change tokens are written to the application log at `info`

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `auth.ts` (reset request) and `me.ts` (email-change) · **Found by:** CC, WS9A Phase 0.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `fa1859c`, PUSHED September 7, 2026; the `me.ts` half device-proven September 8** · **Priority:** **P1 — fix BEFORE the first deploy, not after** · **Target:** WS9A · **Cross-ref:** **BUG-224** (why the log line exists at all) · D-WS9-181.
- 🔴 **THE MOVE ITSELF CREATES THE EXPOSURE, WHICH IS WHY IT LEADS THIS SET.** The log line reads *"would email this token to the user"* and carries the live token plus its `kiwi://reset-password?token=…` URL. **On Hans's laptop that is a scrollback buffer. On Cloud Run it is Cloud Logging — durable, indexed, retained, and readable by anyone holding `roles/logging.viewer` on the project.** Nothing about the code changes; the blast radius changes.
- ⚠️ **`pino`'s redact list covers `req.headers.authorization` and `cookie` and does NOT cover these custom fields**, and `logger.info` fires regardless of `NODE_ENV`.
- **Fix:** stop logging the token, or add both field paths to the redact list. **The line only exists because there is no email provider — see BUG-224. Fixing that removes the reason for the log line entirely.**
- ✅ **FIXED September 7, 2026 — commit `fa1859c` (LOCAL, NOT PUSHED at the time of writing), WS9A Phase 1.** **BOTH halves shipped: the fields are gone from `auth.ts` and `me.ts` (the mints stay, marked void, with a comment naming BUG-224), AND six field paths were added to `pino`'s redact list** so a future line cannot re-leak by copying the pattern.
- 🔴 **THE GUARD'S FIRST BREAK STAYED GREEN, AND §27.5's GREP IS THE ONLY REASON ANYONE KNOWS.** CC re-added the token to the handler, grep-confirmed it present at line 274, **and the test still passed 2/2** — because the newly-added redact list censored the value, so the emitted record was genuinely token-free. ⚠️ **A guard that cannot fail for the thing it names.** ✅ **Strengthened to assert on FIELD KEYS (which survive redaction as `"resetToken":"[Redacted]"`) plus that the censor marker never fires on this path at all — then both breaks went red:** re-adding the field, and removing it from redact. Restores hash-verified (`a0f2d3e9…`, `13b29c79…`).
- ⚠️ **THE TEST RUNS IN A CHILD PROCESS ON PURPOSE, AND THE REASON IS REUSABLE:** `logger` writes to fd 1 through SonicBoom, so patching `process.stdout.write` in-process observes nothing. `NODE_ENV=production` in the child also drops the pino-pretty worker, which is the production shape anyway.
- ✅ **ONE FIELD BEYOND SCOPE, DECLARED BY CC AND RULED BY HANS September 7, 2026 — `newEmail` STAYS OUT OF THE LOG.** It is not a credential, **but an email address in a durable indexed sink is PII.** ✅ **The diagnostic is not lost — `userId` is retained and joins to the user record.**
- ⚠️ **HANS'S RATIONALE IS FORWARD-LOOKING, NOT ABOUT THIS LINE:** *"auditing, reporting, etc. is important when we get to scaling things more."* **What a log is allowed to contain is an audit question before it is a debugging one, and the cost of getting it wrong grows with the log's retention and reach.** ✅ **Cloud Logging is durable, indexed and retained — the opposite of a scrollback buffer, which is BUG-219's whole premise.**
- 🔵 **THE GENERALISATION, worth applying at every future log line: identifiers that JOIN are cheap and safe; the VALUES they join to are not.** Log the `userId`, never the email.
- 🔴 **THE DEVICE PASS DID NOT EXERCISE THIS FIX, AND THIS ENTRY MUST NOT CLAIM IT DID (September 7, 2026).** Hans ran a **password CHANGE** (`PATCH /api/me/password`) and got a clean line — `Password changed via /me/password` + `userId`, no token. ⚠️ **THAT IS A DIFFERENT HANDLER.** BUG-219 fixed the reset-REQUEST handler in `auth.ts` and the email-CHANGE handler in `me.ts`. **The clean line proves those two handlers were never the ones logging, not that they stopped.**
- ✅ **The fix is nonetheless proven — by the child-process guard, which reads the real emitted record and goes red in both break directions.** ⚠️ **Code-proven, not device-proven. Say it that way.**
- 🔴 **AND THE ATTEMPT SURFACED SOMETHING BIGGER — SEE BUG-224: Hans reports there is NO password-reset link or option on the auth screen at all.** **That is why he could not run the test.** ✅ **CONFIRMED by code read September 7 (auth+email Phase 0) — it is worse than unwired; see BUG-235.**
- ⚠️ **REOPENED IN ONE PLACE — CC FLAGGED ITS OWN OMISSION, September 7: `newEmail` was removed from the email-change REQUEST log in `fa1859c`, but the VERIFY handler still logs it at `me.ts:808`.** **Hans ruled the field out of the log (see above); the ruling was applied to one of two sites.** ✅ **One-line fix; ride it on the next touch of `me.ts`, which is the email block.**
- ✅ **CLOSED September 8, 2026 — `c38a596`: the verify handler's log line is now `{ event: "email_change_verified", userId }`, and it is GUARDED (restoring `newEmail` goes red).** The handoff cited the leftover at `me.ts:808` and the lane found it at `:832` — line drift from the seam wiring above it, not a second site.
- ✅ **DEVICE-PROVEN AT LAST, FOR ONE OF THREE SITES — September 8, Hans's `c38a596` pass.** A real email change emitted `event: "email_change_requested"` · `userId` · `sent: false` · `reason: "send_failed"` — **no address, no link, no token, `userId` retained exactly as Hans ruled.** 🔴 **SCOPE IT PRECISELY, THIS ENTRY HAS BEEN OVERCLAIMED ONCE: the `me.ts` email-change REQUEST handler is device-proven. `auth.ts`'s reset-request handler is NOT (no client — BUG-235) and the `me.ts` VERIFY handler is NOT (needs a delivered link). Both stay code-proven.**

### BUG-220 — A 35 MB request body is parsed into memory BEFORE the auth check, so one anonymous caller costs ~110 MB of server RSS

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `recipes.ts` — the `/recipes/import-image` route orders `imageBodyParser` → `requireAuth` → `importLimiter` · **Found by:** CC, WS9A Phase 0, **measured**.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `fa1859c`, local** · **Priority:** **P1 — before a public URL** · **Target:** WS9A · **Cross-ref:** BUG-223.
- **Measured, not reasoned:** 34,000,018 bytes sent with no credentials → `401 missing or invalid authorization header`, 760 ms, **`rss_delta_mb 193.7`**. **Cloud Run's default instance is 512 MiB, so three concurrent requests from one laptop take the instance down — for free, with no account.**
- ⚠️ **THE LIMITER THAT WOULD HAVE STOPPED IT SITS TWO POSITIONS DOWNSTREAM OF WHERE THE DAMAGE IS DONE.** The fix is a three-line reorder: parser AFTER `requireAuth` and `importLimiter`. ✅ **Re-run the probe after the fix and assert `rss_delta_mb` stays near zero — the measurement already exists.**
- ✅ **Every other body limit is fine and was measured:** the default `express.json()` is 102,400 bytes, confirmed from the 413's own payload.
- ✅ **FIXED September 7, 2026 — commit `fa1859c` (LOCAL, NOT PUSHED at the time of writing), WS9A Phase 1.** `imageBodyParser` now runs AFTER `requireAuth` and `importLimiter`. ✅ **Safe because `requireAuth` reads only headers and `importLimiter` only method/path/ip.** ✅ **And the sweep came back clean: `rg` over `src/` finds EXACTLY ONE raised-limit parser in the whole server** — every other parser is the 100 KB default, so no other route has this ordering.
- 🔴 **THE HEADLINE NUMBER IN THIS ENTRY WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE — CC CAUGHT IT ON ITS OWN PROBE. `193.7` MEASURED THE CLIENT AND THE SERVER TOGETHER.** The Phase 0 probe built the 34 MB body **in the same process as the server**, so `rss_delta_mb` always included the client's own copy. That was tolerable while server-side buffering dominated and **misleading the moment it stopped** — the naive after-fix re-run read `156.0` and looked like a failure. ✅ **Re-measured with the server in its own process (`bigbody-split.ts`), 3 samples each: parser-first 126.6 / 110.1 / 88.7 (median 110.1) → auth-first 12.8 / 32.5 / 13.9 (median 13.9). No overlap.** Corroborated independently: a 5 MB unauthenticated POST went **85 ms → 18 ms**.
- ⚠️ **§27.6 item 5 AGAIN, AND THIS TIME IT WAS CC THAT ASKED WHAT THE NUMBER MEASURED.** The entry, the position block and a message to Hans all carried *"194 MB per anonymous request"* as a server cost. **The server cost was ~110 MB.** The conclusion is unchanged — three concurrent anonymous requests still exhaust a 512 MiB instance — **but the figure overstated it by ~76% and must not be re-quoted.** ⚠️ **A probe that runs the client and the server in one process cannot measure either one.**
- ✅ **THE GUARD NEEDS NO MEMORY MEASUREMENT AND THAT IS THE ELEGANT PART:** an over-35-MiB body with no credentials returns **401 if auth runs first and 413 if the parser does** — ordering-sensitive by construction. A companion case (same body, valid token → 413) proves the parser is still mounted, **so a passing 401 can never mean "someone deleted the parser."** Break restored the old order → red; restore `240c9efc…`.

### BUG-221 — `/healthz` cannot fail, so a revision with a broken `DATABASE_URL` deploys green and 503s every request

- **Date:** September 7, 2026 · **Surface:** `health.ts` (a pure literal) + `index.ts` (boot connect deliberately non-fatal) · **Found by:** CC, WS9A Phase 0.
- **Status:** ✅ **FIXED — `fa1859c`, local** · **Priority:** **P1 — it is the deploy gate** · **Target:** WS9A · **Cross-ref:** D-WS9-181.
- 🔴 **TWO INDIVIDUALLY-CORRECT DECISIONS COMPOSE INTO A GATE THAT CANNOT FAIL.** `/healthz` returns a parsed literal and never touches the database. `index.ts` deliberately does not `await $connect()` and deliberately does not make it fatal — a documented BUG-103 decision, **correct in isolation**. Together: a bad `DATABASE_URL` passes the Cloud Run health check, the revision goes green, takes 100% of traffic, and fails every real request.
- ⚠️ **THIS IS THE HARDEST OUTAGE TO DIAGNOSE PRECISELY BECAUSE THE PLATFORM REPORTS SUCCESS**, and it fires at the worst moments — first production deploy, first credential rotation.
- **Needs a decision, not just a patch:** separate liveness (stays pure) from readiness (proves the DB). ✅ **Ten-minute measurement, no cloud account needed: run locally with a syntactically valid but unreachable `DATABASE_URL` and curl `/healthz`. A 200 confirms it.**
- ✅ **PREMISE MEASURED BEFORE THE FIX, exactly as the entry proposed:** a real boot against `postgresql://…@127.0.0.1:59999/nodb` returned `GET /api/healthz -> 200 {"status":"ok"}` while the log carried `prisma_connect_failed`. **The gate cannot fail. Confirmed, not assumed.**
- ✅ **FIXED September 7, 2026 — commit `fa1859c` (LOCAL, NOT PUSHED at the time of writing), WS9A Phase 1.** `health.ts` becomes a `createHealthRouter(deps)` factory. **`/healthz` is UNCHANGED and still pure** (liveness); new **`/readyz`** runs `SELECT 1` bounded at 2 s and answers `503 {status:"not_ready", code:"db_unavailable", retryable:true}` with `Retry-After: 2`. Same probe after: healthz 200, readyz 503.
- ✅ **NO PROCESS EXIT, AND THE REASONING IS RIGHT: readiness is DESIGNED to flap while Neon wakes a branch, and exiting would convert a normal cold start into a crash loop.**
- ✅ **Four guards, and two of them exist to stop a careless future fix:** 503 on throw · **200 on success (non-vacuity — proves the 503 is not unconditional)** · 503 within the bound on a hung query · **liveness stays 200 while the DB is down, which is the half a careless fix breaks.** Break (`status(503)`→`status(200)`) red ×2; restore `9f367f25…`.
- ⚠️ **The readiness body is deliberately NOT a `@workspace/api-zod` contract — it is a platform probe, not a client response shape.** Do not "fix" that later.
- 🔴 **UNVERIFIED, AND IT GATES THE DEPLOY: the 2 s bound is REASONED, not measured.** Chosen to sit under Cloud Run's default probe timeout and above a warm Neon query. **Nobody has timed a real Neon cold start against it.**
